  1. A checkpoint may carry an optional `handoff` object with the keys `next` (required), `state`, `failed`, `open` and `evidence`.
  2. `--handoff` takes one string of `key: value` segments separated by `;`, the format `--risks` already uses, so an agent that can write one can write the other. Only the first `:` in a segment separates the key, so a value may contain colons; empty segments are ignored; an unknown key or a missing `next` exits 2 and names the accepted keys; the input is bounded to 2,000 characters.
  3. **No ledger schema version bump.** `validate_ledger_version` refuses any version that is not exactly `LEDGER_SCHEMA_VERSION`, including an older one, so bumping to 2 would have made AIKit's own ledger and all 20 registered projects' ledgers unreadable. The change is purely additive instead: the key is absent unless given, every reader uses `.get()`, and an older build ignores it harmlessly.
  4. The packet's text passes through the ADR-173 credential gate with the description and the impact fields, so `--handoff` is not a new way to write a credential into a tracked file.
  5. `--resume` prints the latest packet belonging to the active task — an older one describes a state already moved past — and uses its `next` in place of the generic Next Action line. A task with no packet keeps the old line, so nothing is lost for a task that records none.
- **Consequences**:
  - *Positive*: A resumed agent reads what was done, what failed, what is open and what to do next instead of re-deriving it from a diff. An unanswered user question survives compaction. The cold-start Next Action becomes task-specific. One flag and one parser, consistent with an existing validated format.
  - *Trade-offs*: `aikit checkpoint` and `--checkpoint` gain a flag, and `--resume` gains up to four lines of output against PRINCIPLE-004 R5's 100-line budget; a test pins the report inside it. The packet is the agent's own account of its work: unlike a test or a hash, nothing can verify that `failed` or `evidence` is true, so it is a memory aid and never evidence in the PRINCIPLE-014 sense. `next` is required, which makes a packet that only answers "what failed" impossible to record — deliberate, since a packet with no next action does not help a cold start.
  - *Limitation*: Only 20 checkpoints are retained, so a packet older than that window is pruned with its checkpoint. The plan checkpoint is exempt from pruning; a handoff is not.
- **Reported, not fixed here**: `validate_ledger_version` refusing *older* versions contradicts `knowledge/data_models.md`, which states that only a newer version is refused in place. The two have been indistinguishable because the version has only ever been 1. Any future bump that genuinely needs one must fix that first, or every existing ledger becomes unreadable. Left out of this task under PRINCIPLE-007 R4.
- Cross-principle check: no principle section changed. The mechanisms serve PRINCIPLE-004 R1, R2 and R5 (the cold-start report, bounded), PRINCIPLE-005 R2 (README documents the flag in the same commit), PRINCIPLE-006 (resumption after disruption), PRINCIPLE-011 R5 (exit 2 for a malformed packet), PRINCIPLE-013 R4 (no schema break; the existing migration is untouched), PRINCIPLE-014 R1 (a failing-first test per behaviour) and PRINCIPLE-016 R5 (the fields are written only to `knowledge/data_models.md`).


### ADR-175: The Tier 1 Header Stops Naming Its Own Generator
- **Date**: 2026-10-22
- **Status**: Accepted
- **Context**: The Tier 1 context budget is 1,600 estimated tokens per target, and PRINCIPLE-001's headroom rule requires the universal rules to still fit with a full 1,200-character Tier 1B block, so the real limit is 6,403 characters of rendered text. Measured across all eight targets, the worst (`.agents/rules/principles.md`) sat at 6,400: **three characters free**. That blocks any further Tier 1 text, including the `.agents/skills/aikit/SKILL.md` pointer the kit needs because the operational runbook is auto-discovered by only two of the eight supported hosts and no Tier 1 file references it. The header's first line spent 32 of those characters on ``by `scripts/sync_principles.py` ``, which the same rendered file already states twice: the PRINCIPLE-017 capsule names `scripts/sync_principles.py`, and the next header line names `sync_principles.py --involved`.
- **Decision**:
  1. The Tier 1 header reads ``> Generated from `GUIDELINES.md`; do not edit.`` The generator's name is not repeated.
  2. A test pins the three facts that make the removal lossless: the rendered target still matches `is_generated_target`, it still contains `scripts/sync_principles.py` through the PRINCIPLE-017 capsule, and it still contains `sync_principles.py --involved`. If any of them stops holding, the header has to name the generator again.
  3. No principle section changed, so no cross-principle check set applies. The text lives in `render_tier1_target`, not in `GUIDELINES.md`.
- **Consequences**:
  - *Positive*: The worst target drops from 6,400 to 6,368 characters and the budget margin goes from 3 to 35, without removing any information from the file. Eight host instruction surfaces get one line shorter.
  - *Trade-offs*: The `Generated from ` prefix is a `GENERATED_MARKERS` entry, so the phrase is load-bearing for PRINCIPLE-017 R2's refusal to overwrite a file it did not generate; the test asserts it rather than trusting the wording. A reader who wants the regeneration command now finds it one line down or in the PRINCIPLE-017 capsule instead of on the first line.
- **Does not resolve the pointer**: 35 characters is still not enough to add the runbook pointer safely. Measured candidates: ``Runbook: `.agents/skills/aikit/SKILL.md`.`` overruns by 7; the bare path overruns by 4; `See .agents/skills/aikit/SKILL.md` fits with 1 character to spare and `Runbook: .agents/skills/aikit/` with 4. A one-character margin on the gate that blocks every commit is a tripwire, not a design, and the phrasings that fit are the vaguest. Adding the pointer therefore needs a deliberate budget decision — raising `TARGET_TOKEN_BUDGET`, lowering `MAX_DOMAIN_CHARS` at the adopter's expense, or condensing capsule text — and is left to one.
- Cross-principle check: no principle section changed. The change serves PRINCIPLE-001 R1 (the per-target budget it exists to protect), PRINCIPLE-005 R2 (no documented command, flag or exit code changed, so README is untouched), PRINCIPLE-014 R1 (the output change carries a failing-first test) and PRINCIPLE-017 R2 (all eight targets regenerated; the generated-file marker preserved).


### ADR-176: Tier 1 Budget 1,600 -> 1,750 and a Runbook Pointer in Every Host Surface
- **Date**: 2026-10-22
- **Status**: Accepted
- **Context**: The operational runbook in `.agents/skills/aikit/SKILL.md` is auto-discovered by only 2 of the 8 supported hosts (`.agents/skills/` and its mirrored `.claude/skills/` copy); the other 6 read only their Tier 1 file, so no instruction placed in the runbook reaches them. ADR-175 removed the generator's name from the header and measured that the pointer still did not fit: the worst target reserved the full 1,200-character Tier 1B block inside 1,600 estimated tokens, and the pointer costs about 22 tokens (~90 characters). The user approved raising the per-target Tier 1 budget to 1,750 tokens, which PRINCIPLE-001 R9 requires before `TARGET_TOKEN_BUDGET` moves.
- **Decision**:
  1. `TARGET_TOKEN_BUDGET` is 1,750 estimated tokens, and the advisory 90% warning is 1,575. PRINCIPLE-001 R6, both README statements, the runbook and `knowledge/host_clients.md` state the same numbers.
  2. Every Tier 1 header carries ``> Runbook: `.agents/skills/aikit/SKILL.md` for test, sync, gate and recovery commands.``, rendered from one `RUNBOOK_PATH` constant that also feeds `RUNBOOK_MIRRORS`, so the path has one source.
  3. A test asserts all 8 rendered targets name the runbook and that the file exists, so the pointer cannot silently disappear.
  4. `MAX_DOMAIN_CHARS` stays 1,200; the adopter's Tier 1B allowance is untouched.
- **Consequences**:
  - *Positive*: The runbook becomes reachable from all 8 host instruction surfaces. Measured after the change, the worst target is ~1,302 tokens without domain rules and ~1,613 with a full 1,200-character Tier 1B block: 137 tokens (548 characters) of headroom under the headroom check, against ~9 tokens before the raise. No target reaches the 1,575 warning.
  - *Trade-offs*: Every always-injected file grows by one line (~22 tokens), and the ceiling rises by 150 tokens; the extra headroom is allowance for future Tier 1 text, not context consumed on every turn. The pointer is a generated line, not a rule; hand edits are overwritten like every other header line.
  - *History*: This ADR resolves ADR-175's missing-pointer note; earlier ADRs and the dated `knowledge/principles_audit_ledger.md` snapshot keep their historical 1,600 numbers.
- Cross-principle check: PRINCIPLE-000 no conflict (the ADR, tests and freshness gate keep rule edits tied to their citing principles); PRINCIPLE-002 no conflict (the budget remains a Track C gate); PRINCIPLE-003 no conflict (the consequences state measured tokens, not recalled ones); PRINCIPLE-004 no conflict (the ADR precedes the commit); PRINCIPLE-011 no conflict (no onboarding command, prompt or exit code changes); PRINCIPLE-016 no conflict (the number is synchronized in the living knowledge docs and the map is refreshed); PRINCIPLE-017 no conflict (all 8 targets and the mirrored runbook are regenerated from one renderer); PRINCIPLE-018 no conflict (every Phase 4 gate runs before the commit).


### ADR-177: Remediate the Since-Push Review Findings
- **Date**: 2026-10-23
- **Status**: Accepted
- **Context**: The review of the six commits after the last push confirmed five defects that every existing test and gate missed. `delegate_subagent` scrubbed only the success-text path, so `--format json` and both failure reports embedded raw stdout/stderr and could carry a credential into the caller's context. `status --json` returned 1 only for a ledger error, ignoring `--exit-code`, and `project_is_clean` ignored `ledger_error`, so the payload could say `clean: true` while the text verdict failed. `style_findings` searched the rest of the line for `var(`, so a hardcoded font before a themed color passed the lint. `_git_start` had begun raising, leaving `create_project`'s warning channel and `cmd_new`'s `[WARN]` branch dead while `knowledge/architecture.md` still described a warning.
- **Decision**:
  1. Scrub `proc.stdout` and `proc.stderr` once in `delegate_subagent` before any report is built; the JSON `output` and the failure detail use the scrubbed text, and the success-text path still goes through `answer_text`. Tests cover JSON success and failure, text failure and stderr-only failure.
  2. `project_is_clean` includes `audit["ledger_error"]`. `status --json` honors `--exit-code` when it is passed while `--json` alone stays advisory; tests cover the dirty and clean flag matrix and a corrupt ledger on both surfaces.
  3. `style_findings` checks only the matched font declaration's value, up to the next `;`, for a theme token. Tests cover a literal font with a themed color in both CSS and assignment form, plus the token-valued cases.
  4. `_git_start` returns `None`, `create_project` returns only the project folder, and `cmd_new` no longer has a warning branch. Every call site, including the CLI mock, is updated, and `knowledge/architecture.md` states the raising behavior.
  5. No principle text, flag name, exit-code value or ledger schema changed. ADR-171, ADR-172 and ADR-173 keep their historical text; this ADR records the correction.
- **Consequences**:
  - *Positive*: Credentials cannot leak through the delegate's machine or failure reports; the readiness payload and `--exit-code` agree; mixed-line font literals are caught; the create_project contract matches its behavior and docs.
  - *Trade-offs*: `status --json --exit-code` now exits 1 on a dirty or unauditable tree where it previously returned 0; `clean` is false for a ledger error; `create_project`'s internal return changes from a tuple to a path, so external callers (none in this repository) would need updating. Sub-agent `--format json` output is redacted, which can change a raw tool-output string.
  - *Limitation*: The pattern-based scrub remains subject to ADR-173's limitation; a credential that matches no vendor shape still passes.
- Cross-principle check: no principle section changed. The fixes serve PRINCIPLE-003 R2/R5 (call sites and contract parity), PRINCIPLE-004 R2/R4 (cold-start report and ADR), PRINCIPLE-005 R2 (behavior fixes recorded; README already states the corrected verdicts), PRINCIPLE-009 R2 (one scrub point, one readiness predicate), PRINCIPLE-011 R5/R6 (exit codes and remediation text), PRINCIPLE-014 R1/R2 (failing-first tests, no weakened assertion), PRINCIPLE-016 R2 (architecture, data model and service docs updated) and PRINCIPLE-019 R3/R4/R5 (write and output boundaries).


### ADR-178: An Older Valid Ledger Version Is Upgraded, Not Refused
- **Date**: 2026-10-23
- **Status**: Accepted
- **Context**: `validate_ledger_version` (read) and `save_ledger` (write) each refused any version that was not exactly `LEDGER_SCHEMA_VERSION`. `knowledge/data_models.md` stated twice that only a *newer* version is refused, and PRINCIPLE-013 R4 refuses only "a version the reader does not support" and routes loaded data through `migrate_ledger_schema`. While the version has only ever been 1 the two readings could not be told apart. Simulating the next build (`LEDGER_SCHEMA_VERSION = 2`) against copies of real ledgers: AIKit's own ledger and `tally`'s were refused on read, `save_ledger` refused a v1 ledger on write, and `migrate_ledger_schema` was called zero times — the upgrade path could never run. The refusal is safe (the file stays untouched and the message is clear), so the failure mode is every ledger command stopping until the code is fixed, not data loss. Its reach is every ledger in existence: **27, all version 1** — AIKit's plus 26 project ledgers. ADR-174 put it at "all 20 registered projects", which undercounts; that figure is corrected here because past ADRs are not edited (PRINCIPLE-016 R3). ADR-174 reported this defect and left it unfixed under PRINCIPLE-007 R4; this ADR closes it.
- **Decision**:
  1. One predicate, `ledger_version_supported(version)`, answers for both paths: an `int` from 1 to `LEDGER_SCHEMA_VERSION`. The checks used to be two separate `!=` expressions, which is how they came to contradict the documentation together.
  2. `migrate_ledger_schema` stamps the current version on a ledger whose version is missing or an older supported one, so a loaded ledger always declares the format this build writes. A future schema adds its upgrade steps before that stamp. Both callers check the version first, so a newer version never reaches it and is never relabelled.
  3. `save_ledger` upgrades an older supported ledger through `migrate_ledger_schema` before writing, so a file never declares a version older than the format it holds.
  4. Every existing refusal is kept: `0`, `-1`, `True`, `"1"`/`"2"`, `1.0`/`2.0` and any newer version are refused in place on both paths. `test_invalid_ledger_versions_are_refused` is unchanged.
- **Consequences**:
  - *Positive*: Raising `LEDGER_SCHEMA_VERSION` no longer bricks all 27 ledgers; it upgrades them. The code now says what the documentation and PRINCIPLE-013 R4 already said, and the read and write paths cannot disagree again because they share one predicate.
  - *Trade-offs*: None today. With `LEDGER_SCHEMA_VERSION = 1`, "supported" means exactly 1, so behaviour is identical: all 27 real ledgers were loaded after the change and still declare version 1 with their files byte-identical. The new branches are exercised only by tests that patch the constant to 2.
  - *Limitation*: This makes an upgrade *possible*, not automatic. There is no version 2 yet, so no v1-to-v2 transformation exists; the build that raises the version must add its upgrade steps to `migrate_ledger_schema` before the stamp, or it will relabel v1-shaped data as v2.
- Cross-principle check: no principle section changed. The change brings the code into line with PRINCIPLE-013 R4 as written and with `knowledge/data_models.md` (PRINCIPLE-016 R5), keeps refusals in place without quarantine (PRINCIPLE-013 R4), and carries failing-first tests for the read and write upgrades plus a guard that every refusal survives (PRINCIPLE-014 R1).


### ADR-179: The `--help` Cold-Start Budget Is 250ms
- **Date**: 2026-10-23
- **Status**: Accepted
- **Context**: PRINCIPLE-010 R5 required every CLI to answer `--help` in under 200ms as a fresh process, and `scripts/dogfood.py` checked it with one timed run per entrypoint. `aikit.py` joined that loop with ADR-119; it imports nearly every module to print help, and on the development machine it has a floor of about 155-160ms (25 live samples: median 175ms, p90 217ms, 4 over 200ms). Every other entrypoint stays under 145ms even at its worst. A single sample therefore failed on ordinary Windows process-start jitter: the dogfood track failed in 2 of 4 full Phase 4 sequences, surfaced through three gates at once (the suite's `test_dogfood_run_passes_every_track`, `check_test_budgets.py`, which runs the suite, and `dogfood.py` itself), and once in an isolated `verify_commit.py` run that passed on three re-runs. Measuring every revision back to 10-22 showed the same floor, so no commit caused it. The comment beside `budget_ms` cited medians of 47-71ms from ADR-083, which predate `aikit.py` joining the loop. Three options were put to the user: time each entrypoint best-of-three under the same 200ms, make `aikit.py --help` import lazily, or raise the budget. The user chose to raise it to 250ms.
- **Decision**:
  1. PRINCIPLE-010's capsule and R5 state `--help` under 250ms; `scripts/dogfood.py` checks `budget_ms = 250`; the README command matrix says the same. All eight Tier 1 targets are regenerated; the capsule's length is unchanged, so the Tier 1 budget is unaffected.
  2. R2's separate 200ms threshold ("threads only for steps >=200ms") is a different rule about concurrency and is unchanged.
  3. The `budget_ms` comment now records the measured floor and why ADR-083's medians no longer describe the loop.
  4. `TestRewrittenPrincipleWording.test_rewritten_principles_have_one_reading` pins PRINCIPLE-010's rule text, so its two `--help` phrases change from 200ms to 250ms. That is a rule-text pin following a rule the user changed, not a weakened behavioural assertion; it is named here under PRINCIPLE-014 R3. `TestRuleNumberParity` continues to tie the rule text to dogfood's constant.
- **Consequences**:
  - *Positive*: A red startup track again signals a real slowdown rather than an unlucky sample. Idle, 80 consecutive `aikit.py --help` runs: median 158ms, p95 180ms, worst 204ms, none over 250ms. Under load, 6 full Phase 4 sequences (suite, then `check_test_budgets.py`, then `dogfood.py`, so 18 startup checks) were all green, with the slowest `--help` at 160-183ms; at 200ms the same sequence had failed 2 of 4 times.
  - *Trade-offs*: The rule is looser by 50ms for every CLI, including the five that never needed it. The underlying cause is untouched: `aikit.py` still imports nearly every module to print help, and its ~160ms floor now has about 90ms of margin instead of 40ms. A slowdown in that import chain is now 50ms later to be caught.
  - *Not done*: lazy imports in `aikit_cli.py`, which would lower the floor itself, and best-of-three timing in `dogfood.py`, which would remove the single-sample noise. Either remains available and neither conflicts with this budget.
- Cross-principle check: PRINCIPLE-010 R5 changed (the `--help` budget); R2's concurrency threshold did not. Against the principles that cite PRINCIPLE-010: PRINCIPLE-007 (no conflict; no TODO or stub, and the change stays inside the user's decision), PRINCIPLE-009 (no conflict; no module structure changed), PRINCIPLE-011 (no conflict; its exit 0/1/2 contract and `--help` behaviour are unchanged, only the time limit moved), PRINCIPLE-013 (no conflict; no persisted state), PRINCIPLE-014 (served: the pin and parity tests encode the new number, and the edited pin is named above), PRINCIPLE-018 (no conflict; dogfood remains a pre-commit gate with a now-reliable signal).


### ADR-180: `aikit.py --help` Parses With the Grammar Alone
- **Date**: 2026-10-23
- **Status**: Accepted
- **Context**: `aikit.py` imported `scripts/aikit_cli.py` before parsing anything, and `aikit_cli` imports every module the commands use (`disruption_recovery`, `project_registry`, `project_publish`, `subagent_delegate`, `ui_contract`, `update_knowledge_base`, and through them `check_secrets` and `dataclasses`). Printing help therefore cost the whole graph: about 158ms minimum and 165ms median on the development machine, the reason ADR-179 raised the `--help` budget to 250ms. PRINCIPLE-010 R5 already asks a CLI to import only the standard library at top level and anything else after argument dispatch. `-X importtime` showed no single hog (each first-party module costs 2-3ms of self time), so only not loading the command layer could help. `build_parser` needed just `argparse`, `Path`, its own `TriadArgumentParser`, `ROOT_DIR` and `TEMP_STALE_SECONDS`.
- **Decision**:
  1. `report_error`, `TriadArgumentParser` and `build_parser` move verbatim, by line range rather than retyping, to `scripts/aikit_parser.py`, which imports only the standard library at top level. `ProjectError` is imported inside `TriadArgumentParser.error`, the one path that raises it, and under `TYPE_CHECKING` for the annotation. `aikit_parser` never imports `aikit_cli`, so the import-cycle audit, which also counts imports inside functions, stays green.
  2. `aikit_cli` re-exports the three names, so `from scripts.aikit_cli import build_parser` and every one of the 66 test patches on `scripts.aikit_cli` keep working unchanged.
  3. `aikit.py` parses with `aikit_parser` alone when `-h` or `--help` appears in its arguments; help and usage errors exit there. Any command line that gets past that parse falls through to `aikit_cli.main`, which parses it again, so the outcome is the same either way.
  4. `aikit_parser` mirrors `ROOT_DIR` (the same `Path(__file__).resolve().parent.parent` nine other modules already define) and `TEMP_STALE_SECONDS = 60` instead of importing `disruption_recovery`. A unit test pins both to the originals, another fails if `aikit_parser` gains a top-level `scripts` import, and an integration test checks under `-X importtime` that `--help` never loads `scripts.aikit_cli` while a real command still does.
  5. The README gate (`audit_readme` in `update_knowledge_base.py`) found the `aikit.py` subcommands by scanning `scripts/aikit_cli.py`'s source; it now scans `scripts/aikit_parser.py`, where they are declared. The first Phase 4 run caught this: with the declarations moved, every documented `python aikit.py <command>` read as undeclared. As before, a repository without that file (an adopter project) skips the check; a rename of the grammar file would therefore disable it silently, a pre-existing weakness this ADR does not change.
- **Consequences**:
  - *Positive*: 50 help and usage-error forms of `aikit.py` (top level, every subcommand with `-h` and `--help`, every `ui` action, and malformed lines such as `bogus --help`, `checkpoint app --desc -h` and `-- --help`) were captured before the change and after: exit code, stdout and stderr all byte-identical. `aikit.py --help` now measures 123ms minimum and 133ms median (40 runs), down from about 158 and 165.
  - *Trade-offs*: The saving is about 35ms, not the roughly 80ms first projected. The projection compared against a process that only imported `argparse`, but the remaining cost is Python 3.14's own `argparse` pulling in `dataclasses`, `inspect`, `_colorize` and `shutil` (about 35ms), plus constructing a 14-command parser (about 45ms). Going further would mean building only the subparser a given help screen needs, which is more machinery than the gain justifies. `ROOT_DIR` and `TEMP_STALE_SECONDS` now each have one more definition, guarded by the parity test. A help request is parsed twice when it reaches `main`, which happens only for a line where `-h` is not actually the help option, such as after `--`.
- Cross-principle check: no principle section changed. The change brings `aikit.py`'s help path in line with PRINCIPLE-010 R5's top-level-import clause, keeps PRINCIPLE-011 R5's exit codes and output byte-identical, adds no import cycle (PRINCIPLE-009 R5), lists the new module in the README tree in the same commit (PRINCIPLE-005 R2, R5), and carries failing-first tests (PRINCIPLE-014 R1).


### ADR-181: Annotations Must Resolve on Every Supported Python
- **Date**: 2026-10-23
- **Status**: Accepted
- **Context**: Commit e18a1b3 (ADR-172) annotated `project_is_clean` and `project_payload` in `scripts/aikit_cli.py` with `Dict` without importing it. Python 3.14, the only interpreter on the development machine, evaluates annotations only when they are read (PEP 649), so every local test and gate passed. Python 3.10 to 3.13, which the README states as supported and CI is configured to test, evaluate annotations when `def` runs, so importing `scripts.aikit_cli` raises `NameError` and every `aikit.py` command fails. The defect reached origin in push 9653975 and stayed there. CI caught nothing because it ran nothing: every GitHub Actions job since at least 2026-10-19 reports "The job was not started because your account is locked due to a billing issue", with zero steps executed, so the Python 3.10/3.13 and Ubuntu legs the CI workflow exists for have verified no push in that time. Two of the three ways a 3.10 floor can be broken on a newer interpreter were already guarded locally: syntax, by the AST gate parsing with `feature_version=MIN_PYTHON`, and standard-library APIs, by `test_no_standard_library_apis_newer_than_the_minimum`. Deferred annotation evaluation was not.
- **Decision**:
  1. `scripts/aikit_cli.py` imports `Dict`.
  2. `TestAnnotationsResolveOnEverySupportedPython.test_every_annotation_in_scripts_names_something_defined` imports every module in `scripts/` and reads the `__annotations__` of every function, class and method defined there. Reading them forces evaluation on Python 3.14, reproducing what 3.10 to 3.13 do at definition time; on those versions an undefined name already fails the import. It runs in the unit tier, without spawning a process, in about 6ms. Before the fix it reported exactly the two `Dict` annotations and nothing else.
- **Consequences**:
  - *Positive*: `aikit.py` imports again on every supported Python, and the whole class of deferred-annotation `NameError` is caught locally on any interpreter, which matters most while CI cannot run.
  - *Limitation*: This was verified by simulation, not on a real Python 3.10 or 3.13, because none is installed and CI is down. The guard covers annotations on module-level functions, classes and their methods; an annotation on a nested function would escape it, though none exists today. Runtime differences outside syntax, standard-library names and annotations are still unchecked until CI runs again.
  - *Requires the user*: GitHub Actions stays inert until the account's billing lock is resolved in GitHub's billing settings; nothing in this repository can restart it.
- Cross-principle check: no principle section changed. The change serves PRINCIPLE-003 R4 (platform and version parity, here the README's Python 3.10 floor), PRINCIPLE-011 (clone-and-run: every documented command imports again on the stated minimum) and PRINCIPLE-014 R1 (a failing-first test that reproduced the defect).


### ADR-182: The Secret Scan Reads Non-ASCII Paths
- **Date**: 2026-10-23
- **Status**: Accepted
- **Context**: `check_secrets.py` listed tracked files with `git ls-files`, one per line. Git quotes and octal-escapes any path it considers unusual unless told not to, so `şifre_notları.txt` arrived as `"\305\237ifre_notlar\304\261.txt"`. The scanner joined that string to the repository root, read its suffix as `.txt"`, found it outside `TEXT_SUFFIXES` and skipped the file - without recording a gap, although a skipped surface must never read as clean. Reproduced with one fake token in `notes.txt` and the same token in `şifre_notları.txt`: `--all` reported one finding. The diff-based modes (`--staged`, the pre-commit gate, and `--commits`, the publish and auto-push gate) still scanned the content, because they read added lines rather than files, but the quoted `+++ "b/..."` header did not match `+++ b/`, so the finding was labelled `<staged>` instead of naming its file. The defect dates from the scanner's first commit (e94628f, 2026-10-16). Separately, the scanner printed in the console code page when piped on Windows, so even a correct non-ASCII path name printed as replacement characters; only `disruption_recovery.py` and `dogfood.py` switched their streams to UTF-8, although a comment in `dogfood.py` says AIKit's CLIs do.
- **Decision**:
  1. `git_output` runs every git command with `-c core.quotePath=false`, so paths print as raw UTF-8 and diff headers keep the `+++ b/<path>` form.
  2. `iter_tracked_files` lists with `git ls-files -z` and splits on NUL, so no path is quoted and a path's own leading or trailing spaces are kept.
  3. When run as a program on Windows, the scanner reconfigures stdout and stderr to UTF-8 - in its `__main__` block, not at import, so modules that import it keep their own streams.
- **Consequences**:
  - *Positive*: `--all` covers every tracked text file whatever its name, and every finding names its file. `TestSecretScanSeesNonAsciiPaths` checks both against a real repository, and failed first with exactly the reported symptoms.
  - *Trade-offs*: Finding labels for non-ASCII paths change from `<staged>` to the real name, which is the point but is a visible output change. A path containing a newline or a tab is still quoted by git even with `quotePath=false`; `-z` handles it for `--all`, but a diff header for such a path would still fall back to the default label.
  - *Not changed*: `dogfood.py`'s comment remains broader than the code; four other CLIs still print in the console code page. They print AIKit's own text, not user paths, so they were left alone.
- Cross-principle check: no principle section changed. The change restores PRINCIPLE-019 R5's sweep over every tracked file, keeps the scanner's rule that a skipped surface never reports clean (PRINCIPLE-000 R5), serves PRINCIPLE-003 R4 and PRINCIPLE-011 R4 (behaviour independent of the platform's code page and the user's file names), and carries failing-first tests (PRINCIPLE-014 R1).


### ADR-183: A Gate Command With a Line Break Is Refused
- **Date**: 2026-10-23
- **Status**: Accepted
- **Context**: `run_shell` in `scripts/project_gates.py` runs every project-declared command: a project's test command and gate overrides from `.aikit/project.json`, and since the UI guard (ADR-160) the fast and snapshot proofs from `.aikit/ui.json`. On Windows it runs `cmd /d /s /c "<command>"`, and `cmd` executes only the first line of a command containing a line break, returning that line's exit code. Reproduced on Windows: `"exit 0\nexit 7"` returned 0 and `"echo FIRST\necho SECOND"` printed only `FIRST`, while `/bin/sh -c` on POSIX runs both lines and returns 7. A multi-line gate command therefore passed on Windows while skipping its later steps, a gate reporting success for checks it never ran. Neither config loader rejected a line break; both only checked for a string. The behaviour dates from `run_shell`'s first commit (8519ef4, 2026-10-17). None of the 32 project configs in this workspace contains one.
- **Decision**:
  1. `run_shell` refuses a command whose stripped text contains `\n` or `\r`, on every platform and before any process starts, returning `-1` and a message that says to join the steps with `&&` or move them into a script. A refused command is a failed gate, never a pass.
  2. A trailing newline is not a second step: the check strips the command first, so `"pytest\n"` still runs.
  3. Refusing on POSIX too, where multi-line commands work, is deliberate: a project that passes its gates on Linux must not silently run fewer steps on a Windows machine.
- **Consequences**:
  - *Positive*: No project-declared command can report success after running only part of itself, on any platform. One check at the one function every caller uses covers project gates, UI proofs and any future caller.
  - *Trade-offs*: A multi-line command that worked on POSIX now fails everywhere until it is joined or scripted. Translating line breaks to `&&` was rejected because it changes meaning: `sh` runs every line regardless of earlier failures, while `&&` stops at the first.
  - *Limitation*: The refusal happens when the command runs, not when the config is loaded, so `aikit check` reports it as a failed gate with exit 1 rather than as a configuration error with exit 2.
- Cross-principle check: no principle section changed. The change serves PRINCIPLE-003 R4 and PRINCIPLE-011 R4 (one behaviour on every platform), PRINCIPLE-000 R5 (no gate passes without running its checks) and PRINCIPLE-014 R1 (a failing-first unit test that also proves no process starts, and an integration test that a trailing newline still runs).


### ADR-184: The README Command Check Cannot Switch Itself Off
- **Date**: 2026-10-23
- **Status**: Accepted
- **Context**: `audit_readme` checks each documented `python aikit.py <command>` against the subcommands `scripts/aikit_parser.py` declares (ADR-180). When that file was absent it skipped the check silently, which is right for an adopter project, whose README may document `aikit.py` commands that run from the AIKit folder, but meant a rename of the grammar file in AIKit itself would switch the check off without a word. ADR-180 recorded this as a pre-existing weakness and left it.
- **Decision**: When the README documents `python aikit.py` commands and the audited repository has `aikit.py` at its root but no `scripts/aikit_parser.py`, `audit_readme` reports an error instead of skipping. A repository without its own `aikit.py`, such as an adopter project, still skips.
- **Consequences**: In AIKit, renaming or deleting the grammar file now fails `update_knowledge_base.py --check` rather than quietly disabling it. AIKit's README and the READMEs of three projects checked by hand (`tally`, `lattice`, `neurocore`) pass unchanged. A unit test covers each branch.
- Cross-principle check: no principle section changed. The change keeps PRINCIPLE-005 R3's command-parity check enforced and follows PRINCIPLE-000 R5 (a gate must not pass by not running); it carries a failing-first test (PRINCIPLE-014 R1).


### ADR-185: `aikit check` Pushes Only on Request
- **Date**: 2026-10-23
- **Status**: Accepted
- **Context**: `aikit check <name>` has pushed unpushed commits to the project's `origin` whenever its gates passed and the tree was clean, unless `--no-push` was given (commit 339f3ee, 2026-10-17). The runbook (`.agents/skills/aikit/SKILL.md`) makes `check` a project's Phase 4, and no document mentioned the push or `--no-push`; the README described `check` as tests, configured checks, hygiene, guidelines and secrets. ADR-162 (2026-10-22) then made a push conditional on *push delivery authorization* under PRINCIPLE-018 R4, but did not change `check`. An agent on a host that allows a push only on request, following the runbook, therefore published commits without that request. The code cannot know whether a push is authorized; only the agent can.
- **Decision**:
  1. `check` never pushes by default. `--push` asks for it: once every gate passes and the tree is clean it pushes unpushed commits to `origin`, never forced, after the same secret scan as before. On a dirty tree it still skips the push and says why.
  2. `--no-push` stays accepted so existing scripts keep running; it is now the default. `--push` and `--no-push` together are a usage error (exit 2).
  3. The README, the runbook (which now says `check` never pushes and to add `--push` only when push delivery is authorized), `knowledge/services.md` and the flag help all say so.
- **Consequences**:
  - *Positive*: Running Phase 4 can no longer publish as a side effect; whether to push is decided by the agent under PRINCIPLE-018 R4, where ADR-162 put it, and asked for explicitly.
  - *Trade-offs*: A behaviour change to a documented command. An agent or script that relied on `check` to push now has to add `--push` or use `aikit publish`; nothing fails, the push just does not happen, and the command prints no push report.
  - *Tests changed, named under PRINCIPLE-014 R3*: `test_check_triggers_auto_push_unless_opted_out` is replaced by `test_check_pushes_only_when_asked`, which inverts its first case (plain `check` must not push) and adds `--push` and the conflicting-flags case. `test_dirty_check_skips_auto_push_and_strict_fails`, `test_conflicted_check_skips_auto_push_and_strict_fails` and `test_open_ui_task_check_skips_auto_push_and_strict_fails` now run their non-strict case with `--push`: they asserted the dirty-tree warning that only appeared because plain `check` attempted a push, and on the path that can still push they keep checking the same guarantee, that a dirty tree never pushes and says why. No assertion was dropped.
- Cross-principle check: no principle section changed. The change aligns the tool with PRINCIPLE-018 R1 and R4 as ADR-162 wrote them, updates every surface that documents `check` in the same commit (PRINCIPLE-005 R2), keeps PRINCIPLE-011 R5's exit codes (2 for the conflicting flags), and carries failing-first tests (PRINCIPLE-014 R1, R3).


### ADR-186: A Failing Commit's Fix Fits Where the Commit Is
- **Date**: 2026-10-24
- **Status**: Accepted
- **Context**: `verify_commit.py` (ADR-149) ends a failing verification with the undo PRINCIPLE-018 R5 names for an unpushed commit: `git reset --soft HEAD^`. It printed that line for every failing commit. With `--ref` it verifies any commit, and verifying `83b67de` - already on origin, with HEAD at `7003657` - printed it too: following it would have undone `7003657`, a different commit that passes, and the failing one was not HEAD to begin with. The "while the commit is unpushed" qualifier left the reader to notice the mismatch.
- **Decision**: `recovery_fix` chooses the Fix line. A commit that is an ancestor of `HEAD@{u}` is on the remote: add what is missing in a new commit, never rewrite it. A commit that is not HEAD: undoing HEAD would remove a different commit, so add what is missing in a new commit. Only an unpushed HEAD keeps the `git reset --soft HEAD^` advice. The non-HEAD and pushed messages do not print a reset command at all.
- **Consequences**: The advice is safe to follow wherever the commit is; exit codes and the gates are unchanged. Verifying the real `83b67de` now prints the fix-forward line. Without an upstream the pushed check fails closed to "not pushed", so a HEAD with no upstream keeps the reset advice, as before. Tests cover the pushed and not-HEAD branches, both failing first, and the unpushed-HEAD test keeps its assertion.
- Cross-principle check: no principle section changed. The change applies PRINCIPLE-018 R5 (non-destructive recoil, never rewriting published history) to the gate's own advice, keeps PRINCIPLE-011's error triad, and carries failing-first tests (PRINCIPLE-014 R1).


### ADR-187: A Requested Push That Does Not Happen Exits 1
- **Date**: 2026-10-24
- **Status**: Accepted
- **Context**: ADR-185 made `aikit check` push only with `--push` but kept the old reporting: a skipped push (dirty tree) or a refused one (the secret scan found a credential, the remote rejected it) printed `[WARN]` and exited 0. While the push was an implicit side effect, a warning was defensible; once it is an explicit request, a caller that passed `--push` reads exit 0 as "pushed". Reproduced with the push mocked: a push refused because a credential was in the commits exited 0. The three dirty-tree tests ADR-185 adapted asserted exit 0 with `--push`, so they pinned that behaviour, and no test covered a refused push. Separately, verifying the nine commits since the last push showed that `aikit fix`, which ends in `cmd_check(args)` and had no `--no-push` flag, pushed on every clean run before ADR-185 with no way to opt out - proved by running origin's own code with the push mocked - and that ADR-185 stopped it without saying so.
- **Decision**:
  1. With `--push`, a dirty tree prints the Error/Why/Fix triad and exits 1, and a push `auto_push_project` refuses or that fails exits 1 with that function's own triad on stderr. A completed push, or no push needed, exits 0 as before.
  2. `aikit fix` never pushes: it has no `--push` flag, and a test now pins that.
- **Consequences**:
  - *Positive*: Exit 0 after `check --push` means the push happened or was not needed; anything else is a failure with its reason.
  - *Tests changed, named under PRINCIPLE-014 R3*: `test_dirty_check_skips_auto_push_and_strict_fails`, `test_conflicted_check_skips_auto_push_and_strict_fails` and `test_open_ui_task_check_skips_auto_push_and_strict_fails` expect exit 1 instead of 0 for their `--push` case; each still asserts the skip message and that nothing was pushed. `test_check_pushes_only_when_asked` gains the refused-push case and the `fix` case. No assertion was dropped.
  - *Correction to ADR-185*: its trade-off said a script relying on the old push would see "nothing fails, the push just does not happen"; it omitted `aikit fix`, which was the worse case because it could not be opted out.
- Cross-principle check: no principle section changed. The change applies PRINCIPLE-011 R5 (1 for an operational failure) and PRINCIPLE-018 R4 (a push happens only on request, and a request that fails is reported), and carries failing-first tests (PRINCIPLE-014 R1, R3).


### ADR-188: Corrections to ADR-181, ADR-182 and ADR-186, and the Checks That Make Them True
- **Date**: 2026-10-24
- **Status**: Accepted
- **Context**: Verifying the nine commits since the last push found four claims in this register, or in commit messages, that the code does not bear out. Past ADRs are not edited (PRINCIPLE-016 R3), so they are corrected here.
  1. ADR-181, and the message of a0e310d, say the AST gate's `feature_version=(3, 10)` parse guards syntax for the 3.10 floor and that "all three ways" a newer interpreter can mask a broken floor are checked. On Python 3.14, `ast.parse('f"{x["a"]}"', feature_version=(3, 10))` is accepted, although reusing an f-string's quote inside `{}` (PEP 701) is a SyntaxError on 3.10 and 3.11. Module-level annotations were also outside the annotation guard: 3.14 defers them, 3.10-3.13 evaluate them at import, and the guard read only functions, classes and methods. Neither gap hides a live defect today: none of the 729 f-strings in `scripts/`, `tests/` and `aikit.py` uses a 3.12-only form, and all 15 module-level annotations resolve.
  2. ADR-182 says "four other CLIs" print in the console code page and that "they print AIKit's own text, not user paths". It is six (`check_project_hygiene`, `check_reachability`, `check_test_budgets`, `sync_principles`, `update_knowledge_base`, `verify_commit`), and at least `check_project_hygiene` prints a project's own file paths: `şifre_araç.py` reached `aikit check` as `�ifre_ara�.py`. ADR-189 addresses the ones that print user paths.
  3. ADR-186, and the message of 5bbb4cd, say that without an upstream the pushed check "fails closed". It failed open: a commit on `origin/main` pushed with `git push origin HEAD` (no `-u`) has no `HEAD@{u}`, was judged unpushed, and was told to `git reset --soft HEAD^`. Reproduced against a real bare remote.
  4. The message of 7003657 and ADR-185 omit that the change also stopped `aikit fix` from pushing; ADR-187 records that.
- **Decision**:
  1. `recovery_fix` treats a commit as published when `HEAD@{u}` contains it or any remote-tracking branch does (`git branch -r --contains`). Against the real bare remote the pushed commit now gets the fix-forward advice and a local-only HEAD keeps the reset advice.
  2. `TestAnnotationsResolveOnEverySupportedPython` also reads each module's own `__annotations__`.
  3. `TestFstringsParseOnPython310` reads every f-string in `scripts/`, `tests/` and `aikit.py` and fails on a reused quote, a backslash or a `#` inside `{}`. It first proves itself: it must flag two seeded 3.12-only forms and must not flag a 3.10-valid one. It slices the source by the AST's byte offsets from one line split, because `ast.get_source_segment` re-split the whole source per call and took about 2 seconds over the tests.
- **Consequences**: The 3.10 floor is checked locally for syntax the AST gate misses, for module-level annotations and for the advice that could rewrite a published commit. The unit tier averages 6.00ms per test with the new checks, against its 10ms ceiling. A remaining limit: none of this runs on a real Python 3.10, which only CI or a local 3.10 install would do.
- Cross-principle check: no principle section changed. The corrections follow PRINCIPLE-016 R3 (append, never edit) and PRINCIPLE-000 R5 (a check must not claim coverage it lacks); the fixes serve PRINCIPLE-003 R4 (the stated Python floor), PRINCIPLE-018 R5 (never rewrite published history) and PRINCIPLE-014 R1 (a failing-first test for the advice, self-checking guards).


### ADR-189: Reported Paths Are Real Paths, and a NUL Is Refused
- **Date**: 2026-10-24
- **Status**: Accepted
- **Context**: Three defects next to code changed since the last push, each reproduced. The ledger audit parses `git status --porcelain` (v1), which quotes a path holding a space or a non-ASCII letter C-style: of `şifre_notları.txt`, `with space.txt` and `plain.txt`, the audit named only `plain.txt` correctly, so `--resume`, `--audit`, `aikit status` and the `disruption` object of `status --json` reported files that do not exist. The clean/dirty verdicts stayed correct because a quoted name still fills the list. `core.quotePath=false` alone would have fixed two of three names, since git still quotes the space. Second, a JSON `\u0000` escape in a project's `test_command` decodes to NUL, and `run_shell` handed it to `Popen`, which raised an uncaught `ValueError`, so `aikit check` ended in a traceback. Third, `check_project_hygiene.py`, `update_knowledge_base.py` and `verify_commit.py` print a project's own paths in the console code page when piped on Windows, and `aikit check` reads gate output as UTF-8: `şifre_araç.py` arrived as `�ifre_ara�.py` (ADR-188 corrects ADR-182's claim that these CLIs print only AIKit's own text).
- **Decision**:
  1. `unquote_git_path` decodes git's C-style quoting - the named escapes and octal byte escapes, collected as bytes and decoded as UTF-8 - and `classify_git_status` applies it to each path, to each side of a rename or copy separately. An unquoted path is unchanged.
  2. `run_shell` refuses a command containing NUL with exit -1 and a message, as it already refuses a line break (ADR-183).
  3. The three CLIs reconfigure stdout and stderr to UTF-8 on Windows in their `__main__` blocks, not at import.
- **Consequences**:
  - *Positive*: Every path the audit reports exists on disk, spaces and non-ASCII names included; no project configuration can crash `aikit check`; a non-ASCII path in a project gate's finding reaches the user intact. Tests cover each: a pure parser test on git's own quoting forms, an end-to-end audit on a real repository, the NUL refusal without a process starting, and the hygiene scan read as `aikit check` reads it.
  - *Trade-offs*: The parser still reads v1 porcelain, where a path that itself contains ` -> ` inside a rename is ambiguous; git's documentation directs such cases to `-z`, which would mean rewriting the parser and its tests. `status --json` output changes for quoted paths, from git's quoted form to the real name.
  - *Not changed*: `check_reachability`, `check_test_budgets` and `sync_principles` print only AIKit's own text and keep the console code page.
- Cross-principle check: no principle section changed. The change serves PRINCIPLE-003 R4 and PRINCIPLE-011 R4 (behaviour independent of file names and code page), PRINCIPLE-011 R6 (errors as a triad, never a traceback) and PRINCIPLE-014 R1 (failing-first tests, one against a real repository).


### ADR-190: The Ledger Audit Reads NUL-Separated Status; `check --push` Help States Its Exit
- **Date**: 2026-10-24
- **Status**: Accepted
- **Context**: ADR-189 unquoted git's v1 porcelain paths but left one case open: v1 writes a rename as `old -> new` in one field, so a rename whose own names contain ` -> ` cannot be split unambiguously. Windows forbids `>` in file names, so the case arises only on POSIX. Separately, reviewing in full the two help forms that changed since the last push showed that `check --push`'s help and the README still described the behaviour before 2f123c0: neither said that a push is made only on a clean tree or that the command exits 1 when the requested push does not happen. PRINCIPLE-005 R2 requires a change to the conditions under which a documented command exits non-zero to update its `--help` and the README in the same commit; 2f123c0 did neither.
- **Decision**:
  1. `git_porcelain_status` runs `git status --porcelain -z`. `porcelain_entries` reads NUL-separated fields, unquoted, with a rename or copy as two fields - the new path first, as git emits them, confirmed against a real `git mv` - and still unquotes newline-separated v1 text for callers and tests that pass it. `classify_git_status` reads entries from it, and the displayed rename keeps the established `old -> new` form.
  2. `check --push`'s help says it pushes after every gate passes on a clean tree and exits 1 if the push does not happen; the README says the same.
- **Consequences**: The audit parses every path exactly, spaces, non-ASCII letters and arrows included. A test parses a synthesised `-z` rename whose names hold ` -> `; another runs a real rename through the real audit and checks that the adapter asks for NUL-separated output. The displayed `old -> new` string remains ambiguous to a reader for such names, while the parsed paths are exact. This closes ADR-189's stated limitation.
- Cross-principle check: no principle section changed. The change serves PRINCIPLE-005 R2 (help and README follow the exit conditions), PRINCIPLE-003 R4 (one behaviour for every file name) and PRINCIPLE-014 R1 (failing-first tests).

### ADR-191: The Origin URL Is Read as Git Reads It and Never Shows a Credential
- **Date**: 2026-10-24
- **Status**: Accepted
- **Context**: Reviewing pushes 11-20 (`4a53d0a..7e4a939`) re-tested `get_project_repo_url`, added in 339f3ee to read `.git/config` without spawning git. Probed against `git remote get-url origin`, it differed three ways: a key spelled `URL =` (git variable names are case-insensitive) returned None, so `status` printed "none (local only)" for a project that has a remote; `url = <u> ; note` kept the inline comment in the URL; and a quoted value kept its quotes. Separately, a remote URL that carries user-info (a name and token before `@host`) was printed in full by `list`, `status`, `status --json`, the `check --push` report and every `publish` failure, which quotes git's own output. PRINCIPLE-019 R4 forbids a diagnostic that shows a credential.
- **Decision**:
  1. The reader matches the section name `remote` case-insensitively and the subsection `origin` exactly, compares the variable name case-insensitively, and reads the value as git does through `config_value`: quotes removed, backslash escapes taken, a `;` or `#` outside quotes ending it.
  2. `hide_url_credentials` replaces the user-info of every URL in a text with `***`. `get_project_repo_url` returns the hidden form on both its paths, `publish_project` hides the `origin` it reports and the URL of a created repository, and `_triad` scrubs every failure report, git's quoted output included. An scp-style `git@host:path` is left as it is: its user is not a secret.
  3. The module docstring no longer says publish scans every tracked file; it scans the lines the uploaded commits add (ADR-145).
- **Consequences**: `status` and `list` name the remote git uses for the cases above, and no AIKit report prints a credential from a remote URL. The push itself is unchanged: it always ran `git push origin`, so git, not this reader, chose the remote. `include` and `includeIf` sections are not followed; a remote defined only through an included file still shows as none, as before.
- Cross-principle check: no principle section changed. The change serves PRINCIPLE-019 R4 (redacted diagnostics), PRINCIPLE-003 R4 (the displayed state matches the real state) and PRINCIPLE-014 R1 (failing-first tests).

### ADR-192: `check --push` Without an Origin Fails; `new --public` Needs `--publish`
- **Date**: 2026-10-24
- **Status**: Accepted
- **Context**: The review of pushes 11-20 ran `check <name> --push` on a real project with no `origin` remote: it exited 0 and printed nothing. `auto_push_project` returned `(0, "")` for a project without a remote, which was right while 339f3ee pushed implicitly after every passing check, but ADR-185 made the push opt-in and ADR-187 and ADR-190 promise exit 1 when a requested push does not happen; the help and README already said so, the code did not. Separately, `new <name> --public` without `--publish` created a local project and ignored the flag, since only the publish branch reads it.
- **Decision**:
  1. `auto_push_project`, whose only caller is `check --push`, returns 1 with a triad when the project has no `origin`, naming `python aikit.py publish <name>` and `git remote add origin <url>`. A project already up to date, or with no commit, still returns 0: nothing is left to push.
  2. `new` refuses `--public` without `--publish` as a usage error (exit 2) before creating anything; the `--public` help says it applies with `--publish`.
- **Consequences**: A script that runs `check --push` on a local-only project now exits 1, as documented. `test_auto_push_project` expected 0 for that case; the expectation is now 1, which tightens the contract rather than weakening it. A real-repository integration step and a CLI test cover the two refusals.
- Cross-principle check: no principle section changed. The change serves PRINCIPLE-011 R5 (exit 1 on an operational failure, 2 on misuse), PRINCIPLE-018 R4 (a push happens only on request, and a request that cannot be met is reported) and PRINCIPLE-014 R1 (failing-first tests).

### ADR-193: Review of Pushes 11-20; the ADR-133 Key Was Committed to a Public Repository
- **Date**: 2026-10-24
- **Status**: Accepted
- **Context**: Pushes 11-20 (`4a53d0a..7e4a939`, twelve commits, 2026-10-17 to 2026-10-19) were reviewed: each commit's own gates were run in a throwaway worktree, the full history was scanned for credentials, and the publish, launcher, license, hygiene, ledger, delivery and tier-budget changes were read against the current code.
- **Findings**:
  1. ADR-133 says the leaked Gemini key "remains in the history of the private repository". The GitHub API reports `Aegean-E/AIKit` as public with a `PublicEvent` at its creation, 2026-10-15T18:25:00Z, two days before 4a53d0a committed the key. The key has therefore been publicly readable since 2026-10-17, and stays so in history. A scan of every commit, the root included, finds no other real credential; the `sk-ant-` fixture is a fabricated alphabet run.
  2. 5bd8490 and c31f2e6 were pushed red: each added a tracked file the README tree did not list (`scripts/project_publish.py`, then the two root launcher `.bat` files), failing the README drift test, `update_knowledge_base --check` and the dogfood run. 339f3ee and 49e5ee7, the next commits, repaired each. c31f2e6 also added launchers for an unrelated project to AIKit's root; 49e5ee7 removed them.
  3. The remaining failures are ceilings since raised: the integration tier against 200 ms per test (now 350 ms) from 1ae8be7 on, and `--help` at 228 ms against 200 ms in 49e5ee7 (now 250 ms, ADR-179). The open() and timeout hygiene findings in 49e5ee7 to 7e4a939 are fixed at HEAD, whose hygiene scan is clean.
  4. Live defects found and fixed: the origin URL reader and credential display (ADR-191), and `check --push` without an origin and `new --public` alone (ADR-192).
  5. Open: `new <name> --publish` calls `publish_project` directly, so it uploads without the delivery baseline, gates and test-gate check that `aikit publish` enforces (README: "`publish` also runs the project's gates and test gate before uploading"). Gating it as `publish` does would refuse every new project whose stack is not detected, since its seed README keeps a `<FILL:` prerequisite; that trade-off is the user's to decide.
- **Decision**: ADR-133 stays unedited (the register is append-only); this ADR corrects its "private repository" statement. Revoking the key is the user's action and cannot be verified from the repository; history is still not rewritten, since a public history may already be cloned and a force push is never used (PRINCIPLE-018 R4).
- **Consequences**: The key must be treated as compromised until revoked. Item 5 stays open until the user chooses.
- Cross-principle check: no principle section changed. The review serves PRINCIPLE-019 R6 (a pushed credential is rotated, not reverted), PRINCIPLE-018 R7 (each commit verified alone) and PRINCIPLE-003 R1 (claims checked against the real state).

### ADR-194: A Delegated Brief Never Reaches cmd.exe and Is Always the Message
- **Date**: 2026-10-24
- **Status**: Accepted
- **Context**: Reviewing pushes 21-30 re-tested `aikit delegate` (b643039, 79825f7, 4a53d0a) on this Windows machine. `shutil.which("opencode")` returns npm's `opencode.CMD` shim, and Windows starts a `.cmd` through cmd.exe, which re-parses the command line that `subprocess` built. With a harmless fake shim, the brief `a" & echo x > INJECTED.txt & rem "` created the file: a quote in a brief ended the quoting and `&` ran a command. `%PATH%` in a brief arrived expanded, so a brief could carry an environment value, a key included, to the model. Separately, the brief was the last argument with no end of options: the real `opencode run --agent <unknown> "--version"` printed the version instead of treating it as the message, so a brief starting `--auto` would auto-approve every permission the role does not explicitly deny, shell commands included. And `is_free_model` read `opencode/<paid>#-free` as free, testing the `#variant` as part of the model name.
- **Decision**:
  1. `direct_executable` reads an npm `.cmd`/`.bat` shim for the executable it forwards to (`"%dp0%\...\opencode.exe" %*`) and starts that executable directly, so no shell parses the brief. On this machine it resolves to `node_modules\@opencode\cli\bin\opencode.exe`.
  2. When only a script is found, a brief, file or model holding a character cmd.exe interprets (`" % ! ^ & | < >` or a line break) is refused with exit 1 and a triad before anything runs.
  3. `opencode_command` puts `--` before the brief, which the real `opencode run` honours.
  4. `is_free_model` drops the `#variant` before testing the name.
- **Consequences**: A brief reaches the sub-agent byte for byte on every platform, and it is always the message. The refusal in 2 only applies to an installation whose shim names no executable that exists; the `delegate` help states it. Tests use a fake shim with and without its executable, a recorded argv and `#variant` model names, with no process started.
- Cross-principle check: no principle section changed. The change serves PRINCIPLE-019 R4 (no environment value reaches a model), PRINCIPLE-012 R8 (free models only), PRINCIPLE-003 R4 (platform parity) and PRINCIPLE-014 R1 (failing-first tests).

### ADR-195: Review of Pushes 21-30
- **Date**: 2026-10-24
- **Status**: Accepted
- **Context**: Pushes 21-30 (`f7f4cc4..4a53d0a`, sixteen commits on 2026-10-17) built the project system: per-project ledger and decisions, `aikit check` and `fix`, project guidelines, `delegate`, the Claude Code and OpenCode role profiles and the Gemini free-tier provider. Each commit's own gates were run in a throwaway worktree, and the parts still live at HEAD were re-tested.
- **Findings**:
  1. b643039 was pushed red: the README tree omitted the `.claude` and `.clinerules` entries it added, failing the README drift test, `update_knowledge_base --check` and dogfood; c2f49b9 repaired it.
  2. Every other failure is the `--help` startup ceiling of the time, 200 ms (now 250 ms, ADR-179), which the dogfood gate and the test that runs it enforced: `aikit.py --help` at 227-229 ms, `disruption_recovery.py --help` at 215-352 ms. These runs overlapped other gate runs on the same machine, so their times are upper bounds. The tier and hygiene gates did not exist yet.
  3. 4a53d0a is the commit that put the real Gemini key in a test (ADR-133, ADR-193). The secret scanner of that commit passed it, as ADR-133 recorded.
  4. Live defects found and fixed in `aikit delegate`: cmd.exe parsing of the brief through the npm shim, a brief read as opencode flags, and a `#variant` read as part of a model name (ADR-194).
- **Decision**: No further change. The red commits are pushed history and were repaired by their successors; the ceilings they missed have since been raised with ADRs.
- **Consequences**: Pushes 1-30 have now been reviewed (ADR-193 and this ADR for 11-30). Open items remain the user's: revoking the ADR-133 key and the `new --publish` gating choice (ADR-193 item 5).
- Cross-principle check: no principle section changed. The review serves PRINCIPLE-018 R7 (each commit verified alone) and PRINCIPLE-003 R1 (claims checked against the real state).

### ADR-196: A Project Name Is Never a Windows Device Name
- **Date**: 2026-10-24
- **Status**: Accepted
- **Context**: Reviewing pushes 31-50 probed `validate_name` (f7f4cc4), which promises "one portable path segment", with hostile names. Path separators, `..`, dots, upper case and a leading `-` were refused with exit 2, but the Windows device names passed: `new nul` exited 1 claiming that `projects\nul` "already exists", and `new con`, `aux`, `prn`, `com1` and `lpt1` exited 1 because `git init` failed. Nothing was left behind, but the errors named the wrong cause, and on Linux or macOS such a project is created and its repository cannot be checked out on Windows.
- **Decision**: `validate_name` also refuses `con`, `prn`, `aux`, `nul`, `com0`-`com9` and `lpt0`-`lpt9` (the reserved names in Microsoft's file naming rules that the name pattern can produce) with exit 2 and a triad naming the cause. It runs only in `create_project` and `attach_project`, so a project already registered under such a name stays reachable. The `new` help says so.
- **Consequences**: `new` and `attach` refuse these 24 names up front on every platform; names that merely contain one (`conin`, `nul-app`, `com10`) are still accepted. A unit test covers both sets.
- Cross-principle check: no principle section changed. The change serves PRINCIPLE-003 R4 (one behaviour on every platform), PRINCIPLE-011 R5 (exit 2 on misuse, with the true cause) and PRINCIPLE-014 R1 (failing-first test).

### ADR-197: The Benchmark Doc Names the Configured Role Models and Chooses by No Unverified Score
- **Date**: 2026-10-24
- **Status**: Accepted
- **Context**: Reviewing pushes 31-50 compared `knowledge/model_benchmarks.md` (d3a475b, b901896, 0aa51d0) with the role profiles. Its "Specialized Subagent Profile" column and its routing tree put `coder` and `tester` on `openrouter/qwen/qwen-2.5-coder-32b-instruct:free` and `reviewer` on Llama 3.3 or `glm-5.3-flash-free`, while `.opencode/agents/` runs `coder` on `opencode/glm-5.3-flash-free`, `tester` on `opencode/deepseek-v4.1-flash-free` and `reviewer` on `gemini/gemini-3.8-flash`; nothing in the doc said the profiles decide. Two tree branches also justified their pick with SWE-bench scores (49.2%, 39.8%) and two with throughput figures, all marked `unverified` in the table, although the doc's own rule (ADR-114) says an unverified score is never a reason to choose a model.
- **Decision**: The doc states that the column is suitability, that each role runs its profile's `model:`, and that any other model is a `delegate --model` override; a `Configured today:` line names each role's model. The tree drops the unverified scores and throughput figures and says a branch naming another model is an override. `test_benchmark_doc_names_each_roles_configured_model` parses that line against every profile and keeps scores out of the tree.
- **Consequences**: A reader routing a task finds the model that will actually run, and a profile change without a doc change fails the suite. The table's unverified rows are unchanged; they remain unusable as reasons until sourced.
- Cross-principle check: no principle section changed. The change serves PRINCIPLE-009 R6 (sourced model claims), PRINCIPLE-016 R1 (knowledge matches the code) and PRINCIPLE-014 R1 (failing-first test).

### ADR-198: Review of Pushes 31-50
- **Date**: 2026-10-24
- **Status**: Accepted
- **Context**: Pushes 31-50 (`b6e5465..7b7412d`, thirty commits, 2026-10-19 to 2026-10-23) were reviewed: each commit's own gates, the project system, UI review and proof safeguards, post-commit verification, license migration to AGPL-3.0, secret write-boundary scrubbing, cold-start handoff packets, and model benchmark routing.
- **Findings**:
  1. Live defects found and fixed at HEAD:
     - `validate_name` allowed Windows device names (`con`, `prn`, `aux`, `nul`, `com0`-`com9`, `lpt0`-`lpt9`), failing on Windows during directory creation or leaving repositories uncloneable on Windows (ADR-196).
     - `knowledge/model_benchmarks.md` role table and decision tree contradicted the configured role profiles in `.opencode/agents/` and used unverified SWE-bench/throughput figures to justify routing; corrected and pinned by a test (ADR-197).
  2. Gates and budget evolution: `verify_commit.py` was introduced in this push range (9a95501) and strengthened; the `--help` startup ceiling was adjusted to 250ms (ADR-179); UI verification (`ui_proof.py`) and explicit staging checks were integrated into the pre-commit triad.
  3. Open items carried forward: `new <name> --publish` gating tradeoff (ADR-193 item 5) and the OpenCode read-only shell remain open for user direction.
- **Decision**: No historical commit edits. The findings were addressed at HEAD with failing-first tests and verified through the pre-commit gates.
- **Consequences**: Pushes 1-50 have now all been audited. Open items remain for user consultation.
- Cross-principle check: no principle section changed. The review serves PRINCIPLE-018 R7 (each commit verified alone), PRINCIPLE-003 R1 (claims checked against the real state), and PRINCIPLE-016 R1 (knowledge matches the code).

### ADR-199: A New Project Starts AGPL-3.0 Licensed, Described, Tagged and Linked to AIKit
- **Date**: 2026-10-24
- **Status**: Accepted
- **Context**: The user asked that every project `aikit new` starts carry the GNU Affero General Public License v3.0, a proper description of a proper length, tags, and a README mark that it was built with AIKit, leading to AIKit's page. A new project had none of these: no LICENSE, a README whose first section was the quick reference, and nothing for GitHub's description or topics beyond what `publish` could extract from the README.
- **Decision**:
  1. `create_project` copies AIKit's own LICENSE, the GNU AGPL-3.0 text GitHub identifies as `agpl-3.0`, into the project and commits it in the first commit; it refuses with a triad when that file is missing or is not the AGPL text. `_git_start` stages only the seed files that exist.
  2. The seed README opens with a "Built with AIKit" badge linking to `https://github.com/Aegean-E/AIKit`, then the description, lists `LICENSE` in its tree and ends with a License section. `fix`, which also seeds a missing README, adds the badge but not the license lines, since only `new` writes the LICENSE.
  3. `new --description` takes one line of 10-350 characters (350 is GitHub's limit for a repository description); without it the README carries a `<FILL:` marker, so the guidelines gate stays red until the project says what it is, as the prerequisites marker already does (ADR-138). `extract_readme_description` drops the marker, so it is never uploaded.
  4. `new --topic` is repeatable; each topic is lowercased and must follow GitHub's topic rules (letters, digits and `-`, at most 50 characters), `aikit` is always first, repeats are dropped and a repository holds at most 20.
  5. Description and topics are validated before the project folder exists and are recorded in `.aikit/project.json` (`description`, `topics`) for `publish` (ADR-200).
- **Consequences**: A real `aikit new` with a description and topics, its prerequisites filled, passes all four project gates; its first commit holds LICENSE. `attach` adds no license: an existing folder keeps its own. The badge image is served by shields.io; the hygiene gate checks only `.html`, `.css` and `.js` assets, so a README badge is not a hidden dependency of the project's code.
- Cross-principle check: no principle section changed. The change serves PRINCIPLE-005 R1 (a README that says what the project is), PRINCIPLE-011 R5 (exit 2 with a triad on an invalid value, before anything is created) and PRINCIPLE-014 R1 (failing-first tests).

### ADR-200: `publish` Sets the Recorded Description and Topics on GitHub
- **Date**: 2026-10-24
- **Status**: Accepted
- **Context**: ADR-199 records a new project's description and topics in `.aikit/project.json`. `publish` took the GitHub description only from the README's first paragraph, cut to 200 characters, and set no topics, so the tags the user asked for would never reach the repository.
- **Decision**: `publish_project` takes `description` and `topics`. The recorded description is used as given (ADR-199 already bounds it to GitHub's 350 characters); without one, the README's first paragraph is used as before. After the repository is created, or after a push to an existing `origin` while `gh` is logged in, `apply_topics` runs `gh repo edit --add-topic <t1,t2,...>`. A refused topic is reported as a note with the command to retry, with exit 0, since the repository already holds the commits. `aikit publish` reads both from project.json, adding `aikit` when a file from before ADR-199 lists no topics; `new --publish` passes the values it was given.
- **Consequences**: A project created with `--description` and `--topic` and then published gets that description and those topics, `aikit` first. Topics are added, never removed: topics set by hand on GitHub stay. Unit tests drive both publish paths and the refused-topic note through a scripted runner; no real repository is created.
- Cross-principle check: no principle section changed. The change serves PRINCIPLE-011 R5 (a partial success is reported, not hidden), PRINCIPLE-005 R2 (README and help updated in the same commit) and PRINCIPLE-014 R1 (failing-first tests).

### ADR-201: `references/` Is Never Uploaded or Mapped; Root-Ignored Directories Are Skipped
- **Date**: 2026-10-24
- **Status**: Accepted
- **Context**: A `references/Tif/` folder of another project's code (58 files) appeared untracked in the AIKit checkout. The user does not want it on GitHub. `find_python_files` pruned only the fixed `IGNORE_DIRS` names, so the folder's modules entered `knowledge/codebase_map.md` (54 entries) and its import cycles, open() calls and private-attribute writes failed AIKit's structure gate, `update_knowledge_base --check` and dogfood, although git would never track the folder.
- **Decision**: `.gitignore` lists `references/`. `gitignored_top_dirs` reads the root `.gitignore` for lines naming one directory (`name/` or `/name/`, no glob or negation), and `find_python_files` skips those directories at the top level, besides `IGNORE_DIRS` at every level. A name without the trailing slash, a glob or a nested pattern is left to git, so nothing is skipped on a guess.
- **Consequences**: Code kept under `references/` is neither committed nor mapped nor gated, while a project's own nested `references/` package is still scanned. The same rule applies to a project's map: a top-level directory its root `.gitignore` excludes is no part of its catalogue either.
- Cross-principle check: no principle section changed. The change serves PRINCIPLE-016 R1 (the map describes the repository), PRINCIPLE-019 R2 (the ignore list keeps material off the remote) and PRINCIPLE-014 R1 (failing-first tests).

### ADR-202: An Attached Project Shows "AIKit Integrated"; the README Is the One Description
- **Date**: 2026-10-24
- **Status**: Accepted
- **Context**: The user asked that a project AIKit is integrated into carry an "AIKit Integrated" mark, as a project AIKit builds carries "Built with AIKit" (ADR-199). Reviewing the commits since the last push (`c39ccf8..`) found three more things: (1) ADR-199 kept the description twice, in `project.json` and as the README's first paragraph, and `publish` preferred the `project.json` copy, so an edited README would have been ignored and a stale description uploaded; (2) `extract_readme_description` cut at 200 characters, below the 350 that `new --description` accepts and GitHub holds; (3) `attach` accepted one folder under a second name, so two projects shared one `.aikit/` ledger.
- **Decision**:
  1. `attach` inserts `AIKIT_INTEGRATED_BADGE`, linking to AIKit's page, under the README's first `# ` title, or at its top when it has none, through `add_aikit_badge`. The file's own line endings are kept (`write_generated_file(newline="")`), a README that already links to AIKit is left alone, no README is created, and the change is left uncommitted and reported. Without a README, `attach` names `fix`, whose seed README for an attached project now carries the Integrated badge instead of "Built with AIKit".
  2. `.agents/rules/readme_quickref.md.template`, which the copy-into-a-project path pastes into the target's README, starts with the Integrated badge.
  3. `project.json` no longer records a description; `publish` uses the README's first paragraph, now cut at 350 characters. `test_extract_readme_description_finds_first_paragraph_and_truncates` expected the 200-character cut; it now expects 350 and also checks that 300 characters pass whole. The test was changed to match the new limit, not weakened.
  4. `attach` refuses with exit 2 a folder already attached under another name; attaching the same name and folder again stays a no-op.
- **Consequences**: A real `attach` of a committed repository changed only two lines of its README, the badge and a blank line. ADR-199 item 5 and ADR-200's use of a recorded description are superseded by item 3; topics are still recorded and set as ADR-200 says.
- Cross-principle check: no principle section changed. The change serves PRINCIPLE-016 R1 (one source per fact), PRINCIPLE-011 R5 (exit 2 with a triad on misuse), PRINCIPLE-005 R2 (README and help updated in the same commit) and PRINCIPLE-014 R1 and R3 (failing-first tests; a changed test named here).

### ADR-203: Review Since the Last Push (`c39ccf8`); ADR-198 Corrected; the ADR-133 Key Is Deleted
- **Date**: 2026-10-24
- **Status**: Accepted
- **Context**: Before pushing, every commit since `origin/main` (`c39ccf8`) was re-read and run: 3bc33ef and fb9541a (ADR-199, ADR-200), the ledger commit 8d36784, f75d0d7 (ADR-201) and 0851c0e (ADR-202). `c39ccf8` itself and the commits before it were pushed at 11:55 by another agent working in this checkout; that push carried an ADR-198 written by that agent.
- **Findings**:
  1. Fixed in 0851c0e: the description was kept twice and `publish` preferred the stale copy; the README description was cut at 200 of GitHub's 350 characters; one folder could be attached under two names (ADR-202).
  2. Caught before commit, never committed: f75d0d7's first draft annotated `gitignored_top_dirs` with `Set` without importing it. Python 3.14 defers annotations and passed it; Python 3.10-3.13 would have raised `NameError` on import, as in a0e310d. The import was added before the commit.
  3. ADR-198, "Review of Pushes 31-50", is wrong: it gives the range `b6e5465..7b7412d`, "thirty commits, 2026-10-19 to 2026-10-23", which is pushes 1-10, and says `verify_commit.py` was introduced in this range. Pushes 31-50 are `7179ae7..f7f4cc4`, twenty commits on 2026-10-17. What that review found: baa92bd was pushed red (README tree omitted `.opencode` and `opencode.jsonc`; 36a4eb6 repaired it); every other failure was the 200 ms `--help` ceiling of the time; Windows device names were accepted as project names (fixed, ADR-196); the benchmark document contradicted the profiles (fixed, ADR-197); and OpenCode's read-only `explorer` and `architect` can still write through the shell, which their Claude Code versions cannot, while `delegate`'s ledger check (ADR-128) still guards the ledger.
  4. The user reports that the Gemini key committed in 4a53d0a (ADR-133, ADR-193) has been deleted; it is inert, and history is not rewritten.
- **Decision**: ADR-198 stays as written (the register is append-only); this ADR replaces its findings. Each unpushed commit is verified alone, then pushed without force.
- **Consequences**: Open items left for the user: whether `new --publish` runs the gates `publish` enforces (ADR-193 item 5), and whether OpenCode's `explorer` and `architect` lose the shell. The README has 22 characters of headroom under PRINCIPLE-005's 22,500.
- Cross-principle check: no principle section changed. The review serves PRINCIPLE-018 R7 (each commit verified alone before the push), PRINCIPLE-016 R3 (the register corrected by appending) and PRINCIPLE-003 R4 (the oldest supported Python).

### ADR-204: README Condensed Without Loss; "auto-push" Corrected
- **Date**: 2026-10-24
- **Status**: Accepted
- **Context**: The README had 22 characters of headroom under PRINCIPLE-005's 22,500-character limit. The user allowed condensing it provided no information is lost and no text can be read differently by another agent.
- **Decision**: Two sentences that repeat the command matrix are removed, each matched to the row that states the same fact: "`--check` is the read-only mode of `sync_principles.py` and `update_knowledge_base.py`" (both `--check` rows begin "Read-only"), and step 2 of Disruption Recovery's "a task's first plan checkpoint must carry `--why --benefits --risks --mitigation`" (the checkpoint row: "exits 2 when a task's first plan lacks `--why --benefits --risks --mitigation`"). "an open UI task blocks ... auto-push" now names `check --push`: ADR-185 removed the implicit push, and `cmd_check` counts an open UI task as dirty and refuses the requested push.
- **Consequences**: 166 characters saved; the README has 188 characters of headroom. No command, limit, exit code or rule left the README.
- Cross-principle check: no principle section changed. The change serves PRINCIPLE-005 R1 (the size budget) and PRINCIPLE-003 R1 (the text matches the code).

### ADR-205: `new --publish` Needs a Description and Uploads Only the Starter Files
- **Date**: 2026-10-24
- **Status**: Accepted
- **Context**: ADR-193 item 5 left open that `new <name> --publish` calls `publish_project` without the delivery baseline, gates and test-gate check `aikit publish` runs.
