The user accepted the recommendation not to add them: at `new` the project holds only AIKit's generated files and the LICENSE, the secret scan of the uploaded commit still runs, and the seed README's `<FILL:` prerequisite marker would fail the guidelines gate for every new project, so a gated `--publish` could never succeed.
- **Decision**: `new --publish` without `--description` is a usage error (exit 2) before anything is created, so no repository starts without a description. The `--publish` help, the README and the runbook state that it uploads only the starter files and that later uploads go through `publish` and its gates.
- **Consequences**: ADR-193 item 5 is closed. A published starter README still shows the prerequisites `<FILL:` line until the project fills it; the guidelines gate keeps `check` and `publish` red until then.
- Cross-principle check: no principle section changed. The change serves PRINCIPLE-011 R5 (exit 2 with a triad on misuse), PRINCIPLE-018 R4 (only on the user's request) and PRINCIPLE-005 R2 (help, README and runbook in the same commit).

### ADR-206: OpenCode's `explorer` and `architect` Run No Shell Command
- **Date**: 2026-10-24
- **Status**: Accepted
- **Context**: ADR-198 and ADR-203 recorded that OpenCode applies the role profiles' permission lists, but every role starts from OpenCode's `*:* allow` and only named git, `rm`, `del` and `Remove-Item` commands were denied, so `explorer` and `architect`, whose texts say they are read-only, could still write files through the shell (`echo >`, `python -c`, `Set-Content`, `mv`). Their Claude Code versions have no Bash tool. The user accepted the recommendation to deny the shell entirely; a list of allowed commands such as `git log*` was not used, because it could not be confirmed that OpenCode 2.1.1 checks each part of a chained command.
- **Decision**: Both profiles end their permissions with `shell` `*` `deny`; since the last matching rule wins, every command is denied. Their texts tell them to use the grep, glob and read tools, and the architect to ask the lead agent for git history. `sync_principles.py` regenerated their Claude Code copies. In `TestOpenCodePermissionBoundaries`, `test_each_subagent_edits_only_its_own_area` expected every role to be allowed `python -m unittest discover tests`; it now expects `deny` for the two read-only roles and `allow` for the others, and `test_read_only_subagents_cannot_write_through_the_shell` checks six write and git commands. The expectation was tightened, not weakened.
- **Consequences**: Read back from the installed OpenCode (`opencode debug agents`), both roles resolve `shell:*` to deny after every allow, with only OpenCode's own `browser` deny after it; `read` stays allowed and `coder` is unchanged. The two roles lose `git grep` and `git log`, as their Claude Code versions already do.
- Cross-principle check: no principle section changed. The change serves PRINCIPLE-008 R6 (sub-agent bounds), PRINCIPLE-003 R4 (one behaviour on both hosts) and PRINCIPLE-014 R1 and R3 (failing-first tests; a changed test named here).

### ADR-207: The Project Gates Are No Sandbox, and AIKit Says So
- **Date**: 2026-10-24
- **Status**: Accepted
- **Context**: An external review noted that `check`, `fix` and `publish` run a project's test command and configured gates under the user's own account: process supervision and timeouts bound them in time, not in what they may read, write or reach. Attaching an untrusted repository and running its gates therefore runs untrusted code, and neither the README nor `attach` said so. The same review found the GitHub description promising a hash-chained ledger and evidence packs the code does not have; with the user's approval the description now names what exists, and the `hash-chain`, `evidence`, `compliance` and `audit` topics were removed.
- **Decision**: `attach` prints that `check`, `fix` and `publish` run the project's own commands under the user's account, unsandboxed, and to attach only trusted repositories; the README states the same. The dogfood jail (PRINCIPLE-015) stays described as a disposable workspace for AIKit's own runs, not as isolation for a project's code.
- **Consequences**: The trust boundary is stated where a user meets it. No isolation is added; containerised gate runs remain a possible later option. The README has 2 characters of headroom.
- Cross-principle check: no principle section changed. The change serves PRINCIPLE-011 R5 (a user is told what a command does), PRINCIPLE-019 (security boundaries are explicit) and PRINCIPLE-014 R1 (failing-first test).

### ADR-208: Review Records Are Indexed, Not Moved Out of the Register
- **Date**: 2026-10-24
- **Status**: Accepted
- **Context**: An external review found the 483 KB register hard to navigate because decisions and review records are mixed, and suggested moving review records to a separate log. The plan was to move the bodies of the review ADRs into `knowledge/review_log.md`, leaving a pointer in each. PRINCIPLE-016 R3 forbids it: an ADR "MAY be edited until the commit that adds it; after that its text is fixed".
- **Decision**: No committed ADR text changes. `knowledge/INDEX.md` gains a "Review Records" table listing the reviews (ADR-107, ADR-113, ADR-128, ADR-163, ADR-177, ADR-193, ADR-195, ADR-203), the corrections of earlier ADRs (ADR-117, ADR-170, ADR-188, ADR-193, ADR-203) and the incidents (ADR-042, ADR-133), noting that ADR-198 gives the wrong push range and ADR-203 replaces its findings.
- **Consequences**: A reader finds the review history from the index. The register keeps growing with each review; recording future reviews outside it would need PRINCIPLE-016 R3 amended, which is the user's decision.
- Cross-principle check: no principle section changed. The change serves PRINCIPLE-016 R1 and R3 (navigable knowledge; the register's text left fixed).

### ADR-209: Every Ledger Write Names Its Agent; Another Agent's Task Needs `--take-over`
- **Date**: 2026-10-24
- **Status**: Accepted
- **Context**: Another agent working in this checkout picked up this session's ledger handoff, committed its uncommitted milestone under its own messages, marked the task complete twice and pushed (ADR-203). Nothing in the ledger said who wrote what, so the interleaving was found only by reading the reflog. The user asked that each agent record who did what and who continued.
- **Decision**:
  1. `resolve_agent` names the writer: `--agent`, else `AIKIT_AGENT`, else the host Claude Code puts in `AI_AGENT` with the first 8 characters of `CLAUDE_CODE_SESSION_ID` (so two Claude sessions differ), else `unidentified`. Only Claude Code's variables are read, since only they were verified on this machine; every other host passes `--agent`. A name outside `[A-Za-z0-9][A-Za-z0-9._:@-]{0,63}` exits 2.
  2. `record_checkpoint`, `mark_task_completed` and `reset_active_task` store `agent` on every entry and `owner` on the active task. A write to, completion of, reset of, or replacement of an in-progress task owned by a different identified agent raises `LedgerOwnershipError` before anything is written; both CLIs (`disruption_recovery.py --checkpoint/--complete/--reset`, `aikit checkpoint/complete`) print a triad and exit 1. `--take-over` lets it through and stores `continued_from` with the previous owner. An unidentified owner can be claimed without `--take-over`, since nothing tells two unidentified agents apart; the entry still records `continued_from: unidentified`.
  3. `--resume` prints `Owner: <agent> (started by A; continued by B)` for the interrupted task.
  4. PRINCIPLE-006 gains R7, "Agent Attribution & Explicit Take-Over", and its capsule "ledger writes name `--agent`; another agent's task needs `--take-over`", so every host's Tier 1 file carries the duty. Its `--citing` set was checked: PRINCIPLE-002 (checkpoint moments unchanged), PRINCIPLE-004 (the cold-start report gains the owner line; no conflict), PRINCIPLE-008 (sub-agents still never write the ledger), PRINCIPLE-011 (exit 1 on a refused write, 2 on a bad name, triads on stderr), PRINCIPLE-012 (the capsule stays within budget, ~1,319 tokens), PRINCIPLE-013 (the check runs inside the existing ledger lock, so it is atomic with the write) and PRINCIPLE-018 (commit and push rules unchanged); no conflict.
  5. `TestPrinciple006Unambiguity.test_every_rule_names_its_audience` asserted exactly six rules so that the cited R1-R6 keep their numbers; it now asserts R1-R7 in order, which still fails on any renumbering. The change follows the rule added in 4 and weakens nothing.
  6. The README makes room without loss: the directory tree's arrow column moves two places left on every line padded by four or more spaces, the two opening lines are merged with every item kept, "Standard Library only — zero external pip dependencies" becomes "standard library only; no pip dependencies", and the checkpoint row names `--agent` and `--take-over` by pointing to PRINCIPLE-006 R7, whose capsule the README's principle table prints.
- **Consequences**: A second agent can no longer finish, commit under or replace another agent's task through the ledger without leaving `continued_from` behind; on this machine the incident of ADR-203 would have ended at its first checkpoint. Hosts that set no identity and pass no `--agent` stay `unidentified` and can still claim each other's tasks; R7 requires them to name themselves. While this was built, another agent edited this working tree and added the three variable names to `EXTERNAL_SCHEMA_KEYS` in `check_reachability.py`; the change is required (without it the shield reports them as state keys with no producer), so it was kept with a comment.
- Cross-principle check: PRINCIPLE-006 changed (R7 and its capsule); its citing set PRINCIPLE-002, PRINCIPLE-004, PRINCIPLE-008, PRINCIPLE-011, PRINCIPLE-012, PRINCIPLE-013 and PRINCIPLE-018 was checked as above, with no conflict.

### ADR-210: Review of the Agent Attribution Commit (2683d1d)
- **Date**: 2026-10-24
- **Status**: Accepted
- **Context**: Before pushing, 2683d1d (ADR-209) and its ledger commit were re-read and re-run. The guard, the CLI paths and the README were confirmed against real runs: a second named agent is refused with exit 1 on `--checkpoint`, `--complete` and `--reset`, a bad name exits 2, `--take-over` records `continued_from`, a newer-version, corrupt or non-object ledger is still reported or quarantined as before, and the README tree is still aligned. Three defects were found.
- **Decision**:
  1. `checkpoint_command`, the `--complete` and `--reset` paths and `aikit complete` caught `ValueError` around the whole ledger write, so any future `ValueError` inside it would have been reported as an invalid agent name. Only `resolve_agent` is inside that `try` now.
  2. A take-over that replaces another agent's task with a different one recorded only the new agent on the superseded entry. That entry now also carries `continued_from` with the owner it replaced.
  3. `agent_history` began with the first entry that named an agent, so a task begun before ADR-209 was said to be "started by" whoever wrote it later. It now reads the task's first entry and says "started by an unrecorded agent" when that entry names none.
- **Consequences**: Three tests pin these: a `ValueError` from inside `record_checkpoint` propagates with its own message, a superseding take-over names the replaced owner, and a pre-attribution task's history names no false starter.
- Cross-principle check: no principle section changed. The change serves PRINCIPLE-011 R6 (an error keeps its true cause), PRINCIPLE-006 R7 (every take-over traceable) and PRINCIPLE-014 R1 (failing-first tests).

### ADR-211: The Active Task Keeps Who Started and Who Continued It
- **Date**: 2026-10-24
- **Status**: Accepted
- **Context**: A second review of `fd48d2c..dbd8336` (ADR-209, ADR-210) found two more defects. The ledger keeps at most 20 checkpoints and trims a long active task to its plan and its latest entries (`prune_checkpoints`), so the entry that recorded a take-over was dropped and `--resume` no longer said who continued, or named the wrong starter. And `checkpoint_command` printed its missing-impact warning before the ownership check, so a refused write warned about a checkpoint that was never written.
- **Decision**: The active task stores `started_by`, carried forward while the same task stays in progress, and `continuations`, one `{agent, from, at}` per take-over, appended by `record_checkpoint`, `mark_task_completed` and `reset_active_task`; `agent_history` reads them first and falls back to the checkpoints for a task written before this change. The warning is printed only after the write succeeded.
- **Consequences**: The ownership chain survives any pruning: a test takes over a task, writes 30 more checkpoints, confirms the take-over entry is gone, and still reads `Owner: b (started by a; continued by b)`. A task already in progress when this change arrived has no `started_by` and reads "started by an unrecorded agent" until it ends.
- Cross-principle check: no principle section changed. The change serves PRINCIPLE-006 R7 (who continued stays recorded), PRINCIPLE-004 R2 (the cold-start report stays true) and PRINCIPLE-014 R1 (failing-first tests).


### ADR-212: Portfolio-Wide Dual-Licensing and Commercial Framework
- **Date**: 2026-10-25
- **Status**: Accepted
- **Context**: The portfolio spans multiple domains (developer tooling, AI engines, games, and clinical templates). While AGPL-3.0 provides strong copyleft protection against closed-source forks, it does not prohibit third parties from selling copies, nor does it provide a path for commercial organizations to adopt portfolio code in proprietary closed-source applications. Creative and domain assets also required clear non-commercial protection.
- **Decision**:
  1. Append the Aegean-E Dual-Licensing Notice to `LICENSE`, directing commercial and proprietary inquiries to `aegean-licensing@proton.me` and `COMMERCIAL.md`.
  2. Create `COMMERCIAL.md` establishing the Aegean-E Commercial License (AECL) framework, waiving AGPL-3.0 copyleft obligations for enterprise licensees and safeguarding non-code assets against commercial exploitation.
  3. Update `scripts/project_registry.py` (`seed_commercial`, `render_project_readme`, `_git_start`, `create_project`) so that new projects created via `aikit new` automatically seed `COMMERCIAL.md` and reference dual-licensing in their README tree and license section.
  4. Update `tests/test_kit.py` to assert that new projects are generated with `COMMERCIAL.md` alongside `LICENSE`.
  5. Update `README.md` tree to list `COMMERCIAL.md` while remaining within the 22,500-character ceiling.
- **Consequences**: Enables commercial enterprise licensing across the entire portfolio while preserving open-source community adoption and full regression gate compliance.
- Cross-principle check: no principle section changed. Serves PRINCIPLE-000, PRINCIPLE-005 (README tree completeness and size ceiling), PRINCIPLE-011 (instant usability), PRINCIPLE-014 (100% test pass gate), PRINCIPLE-016 (ADR consecutive ordering) and PRINCIPLE-018 (git integrity).

### ADR-213: Badge Refresh Migrates the Pre-Move AIKit URL
- **Date**: 2026-10-25
- **Status**: Accepted
- **Context**: Commit a4725e5 moved `AIKIT_URL` to `https://github.com/AegeanWorks/AIKit` but every seeded project README still linked `https://github.com/Aegean-E/AIKit`. `add_aikit_badge` only short-circuited on the current URL, so refreshing an attached project appended a second, "Integrated" badge above the stale one instead of repairing the link; `fix` never refreshed the badge at all, and a README holding both URLs kept the stale one because the current URL matched first. It also shipped with a stale codebase map.
- **Decision**: Keep the old address as `LEGACY_AIKIT_URL` and add `migrate_aikit_links(project_root)`, which rewrites it to `AIKIT_URL` in place and preserves the file's line endings. `add_aikit_badge` migrates first, so a mixed README loses the stale link and an already-linked README is never given a second badge; it still returns bool. `attach` reports migration separately from badge insertion, and `fix` runs the migration for every project it refreshes, built or attached.
- **Consequences**: `aikit attach`/`aikit fix` repairs the link instead of duplicating it, and the CLI no longer claims a badge was added when only a URL changed. The portfolio READMEs migrate one repository at a time as each is refreshed, since the tool never writes into a project the user did not name. Failing-first tests cover the legacy rewrite, the mixed-URL case, CRLF preservation, idempotency and the `fix` path.
- Cross-principle check: no principle section changed. Serves PRINCIPLE-000, PRINCIPLE-005 (README badge and link integrity), PRINCIPLE-014 (failing-first test, 100% pass) and PRINCIPLE-016 (consecutive ADR register).

### ADR-214: Personal-Use Default, Paid Commercial Use and a Restricted Tier
- **Date**: 2026-10-25
- **Status**: Accepted
- **Context**: The user wants the portfolio free for personal use and paid for commercial use, wants no AGPL, and wants to stop distributing a project once it becomes too valuable. ADR-212 kept AGPL-3.0 as the free track, but AGPL-3.0 permits commercial use free of charge, so no commercial user ever needed the commercial license. A license already granted cannot be revoked, so withdrawal can only act on later versions. The canonical license texts live in the `aegean-licensing` project (its ADR-012), not in AIKit.
- **Decision**:
  1. AIKit carries pinned copies (refreshed after its ADR-013) of `aegean-licensing`'s `licenses/AL-1.0.md` (Aegean License 1.0, free personal use), `licenses/ACL-1.0.md` (Aegean Commercial License 1.0) and `licenses/RESTRICTED-NOTICE.md` in its own `licenses/`, with their normalized SHA-256 digests in `licenses/PIN.json`. A unit test fails when a copy no longer matches its digest, or when `LICENSE` is not the AL-1.0 copy verbatim. AIKit never imports the `aegean_licensing` package, which is not a declared dependency (PRINCIPLE-003 R3). Versions of AIKit already published under AGPL-3.0 stay AGPL-3.0.
  2. Rewrite `COMMERCIAL.md` around three tiers (Personal, Commercial, Restricted). Since `new` copies it into every project, it links only to `LICENSE`; the ACL-1.0 text travels with each certificate.
  3. `aikit new` seeds AL-1.0 (`seed_license` checks `PERSONAL_LICENSE_HEADER`) and COMMERCIAL.md (`seed_commercial` checks `COMMERCIAL_HEADER`); `render_project_readme`'s `agpl` flag becomes `licensed`.
  4. Add `scripts/project_license.py` and `aikit license <name> [--restrict]`. Restricting fills the pinned `RESTRICTED-NOTICE.md` with the project, date and cutoff commit (`git rev-parse HEAD`), removes `COMMERCIAL.md`, switches the README tree line and License section, and records `license: {tier, since, since_commit}` in `.aikit/project.json` under `file_lock`. A second restrict keeps the first cutoff; a project with no commit is refused before any file changes. Nothing is committed, and the command prints the manual steps AIKit cannot take (make the repository private, remove public releases, tell ACL-1.0 holders).
  5. `aikit publish --public` refuses a restricted project with exit 2, and `check --push` and `publish` to an existing origin refuse it (exit 2, before any gate runs) unless `gh repo view` reports the repository `PRIVATE`; a public, internal or unknown visibility counts as public (`project_license.public_remote_refusal`).
  6. The README tree check (PRINCIPLE-005 R5) requires only tracked files that still exist: `restrict` deletes COMMERCIAL.md, which stays tracked until the deletion is committed, and requiring it blocked the check that must pass before that commit. Two existing tests listed tracked files absent from their simulated disk; their fixtures now put those files on disk, and their assertions are unchanged.
  7. `aikit status` prints a `License:` line (personal, restricted with its cutoff, or unrecorded) and `status --json` carries `project.license` (tier, since, since_commit), so a restricted project is recognisable without `aikit license`. The private-remote refusal tells the user that a remote outside GitHub cannot be confirmed and must be checked and pushed by hand.
- **Consequences**: Commercial users now need ACL-1.0. A restricted project has a recorded, forward-only cutoff, so it is clear which versions keep the earlier license. The pin detects a hand edit in AIKit, but not a change made upstream: after `aegean-licensing` changes a text, the copy and digest must be refreshed by hand. AL-1.0 is not OSI open source and has not been reviewed by a lawyer. There is no command to move a project back out of the restricted tier.
- Cross-principle check: no principle section changed. Serves PRINCIPLE-005 (README within 22,500 characters), PRINCIPLE-011 R6 (Why/Fix on every refusal), PRINCIPLE-013 (atomic writes, locked project.json), PRINCIPLE-014 (failing-first tests) and PRINCIPLE-016 (consecutive ADR register).

### ADR-215: Repository Topics Follow the Detected Stack and Ownership Is Explicit
- **Date**: 2026-10-25
- **Status**: Accepted
- **Context**: `aikit new` records only the topics the user passes (plus `aikit`), and `publish` sent that list unchanged, so a Python or TypeScript project reached GitHub labelled only `aikit` and was not discoverable by language. The GitHub description also still advertised AGPL-3.0 after AIKit moved to AL-1.0. Publishing into an organization was impossible without adding a remote by hand.
- **Decision**: `publish` builds its topic list as the recorded topics, then one topic per detected stack (`python`, `javascript`, `rust`, `golang`, `java`, `dotnet`), then `typescript` for a Node project with a `tsconfig.json` or a `typescript` dependency, then `source-available` for a personal-tier project, without repeats and capped at GitHub's 20. A restricted project gets no license topic. `publish --owner ORG` creates `ORG/name` through `gh repo create`; without it the logged-in account is used. Stack topics are added at publish time because `new` runs before any stack marker exists, and `project.json` keeps only what the user declared.
- **Consequences**: A repository created by AIKit is discoverable by language and license tier without a second manual step. A topic removed on GitHub returns on the next publish because the list is additive. Changing the owner of an existing project still needs a manual remote; `--owner` only decides where a new repository is created. The TypeScript signal is a file or dependency, so a Node project that compiles TypeScript some other way needs `--topic typescript`.
- Cross-principle check: no principle section changed. Serves PRINCIPLE-003 (detected facts, no guess about a stack), PRINCIPLE-009 (one mapping table), PRINCIPLE-011 (Why/Fix on a failed `gh` call) and PRINCIPLE-014 (failing-first tests for topics and owner).

### ADR-216: Creation and Publish Share Description and Topic Rules
- **Date**: 2026-10-26
- **Status**: Accepted
- **Context**: `new --publish` uploaded only the topics the user passed plus `aikit`, while `publish` had learned to add the detected stack and license topics (ADR-215), so the first push of a project AIKit itself created was less discoverable than a later one. Descriptions were only length-checked at `new`; `publish` sent whatever the README held, including an empty or lower-case paragraph, and an existing project could be published undescribed. ADR-202 keeps the README as the one description source, so the fix belongs in how that paragraph is read, not in a second stored copy.
- **Decision**:
  1. `capitalize_sentences` turns a description into sentence case: the first letter and the first letter after `.`, `!` or `?` (followed by whitespace) are upper case, everything else is untouched, and a known dotted abbreviation (`e.g.`, `i.e.`, `etc.`, `u.s.`, `p.m.`, ...) never capitalizes the next word. `validate_description` applies it before the 10-350 character and single-line checks, so `new` stores and renders the cased text.
  2. `extract_readme_description` applies the same casing and GitHub's 350-character cut, so `publish` uploads exactly what the README shows.
  3. `cmd_publish` reads that description before any audit or gate and refuses an existing project below 10 characters (exit 2, Why/Fix), passing the validated text to `publish_project`; `new --publish` is already protected by `--description`. A repository is therefore never created undescribed.
  4. `cmd_new --publish` uses `publish_topics(root, ...)`: user topics, `aikit`, the language a known test runner names (`python`, `npm`/`jest`, `cargo`, `go test`, `mvn`/`gradle`, `dotnet`) and `source-available` for the seeded AL-1.0 license. The folder is empty at creation, so the remaining stack and TypeScript topics arrive on the next publish, when marker files exist. `publish_topics` now takes the project folder rather than a `Project`, so creation can call it before the registry entry is read back.
- **Consequences**: First-push metadata matches later publishes for everything creation can know. A project whose README has no real first paragraph can no longer be published until one is written. Sentence casing changes only the case of the user's wording; it never rewrites words. Dotted abbreviations outside the known set can still capitalize the following word, which the author can correct in the README.
- Cross-principle check: no principle section changed. Serves PRINCIPLE-009 (one detector and one casing rule), PRINCIPLE-005 (README remains the description source), PRINCIPLE-011 (triad on the new refusal), PRINCIPLE-014 (failing-first tests) and PRINCIPLE-016 (consecutive ADR register).

### ADR-217: Restricted README Rewrites and Status Survive Missing Data
- **Date**: 2026-10-26
- **Status**: Accepted
- **Context**: An independent review of the last eight pushes found two edge cases in the restricted tier. `_restricted_readme` replaced a LICENSE tree entry only when it began with `├──`, so a README whose LICENSE was the last entry (`└──`) kept a line describing AL-1.0 after `restrict`. `license_summary` printed `since None, cutoff  (All Rights Reserved)` when `project.json` recorded the tier without its cutoff, which a hand edit or a partial older record can produce.
- **Decision**: The README rewrite matches either tree prefix and keeps the one the file uses; the COMMERCIAL.md entry is removed the same way. The status line names an unrecorded date or commit instead of printing empty values. Failing-first tests cover a last-entry LICENSE and a tier record without its cutoff.
- **Consequences**: Restricting a project always leaves the README describing All Rights Reserved, whatever position the tree entry holds. A damaged license record is reported honestly instead of looking like a date of `None`. The other findings of that review (creation metadata, sentence casing, register statuses) are ADR-216 here and ADR-019 in aegean-licensing.
- Cross-principle check: no principle section changed. Serves PRINCIPLE-005 (README describes the real license), PRINCIPLE-011 (no misleading output) and PRINCIPLE-016 (consecutive ADR register).

### ADR-218: Three Personal Licenses, Title Case, and AI-Chosen License and Topics
- **Date**: 2026-10-26
- **Status**: Accepted
- **Context**: ADR-216 sentence-cased descriptions, which is not what the user asked: the capital rule belongs to titles. AIKit also had one personal license, so games and science projects were forced into AL-1.0 or hand-edited. Creation knew nothing about the domain, and topics were limited to user input plus detected stacks.
- **Decision**:
  1. `title_case` title-cases a display title (project name, README H1): significant words capitalised, small joining words lower unless first or last, known acronyms preserved (`muse-ai` -> `Muse AI`). Descriptions keep the author's case. `render_project_readme` uses the title; `validate_description` no longer changes case.
  2. `LICENSE_CHOICES` maps `software` to AL-1.0, `game` to AGL-1.0 (DRM-free personal play) and `science` to ASL-1.0 (non-commercial research). `new --kind` seeds the chosen text and README section; `license --set <kind>` switches it on a personal project and refuses a restricted one.
  3. `detect_license_kind` scores strong and weak markers (`project.godot`, `*.uproject`, `Assets/`; `*.ipynb`, `paper.tex`, `CITATION.cff`, `notebooks/`). `license --suggest` reports the kind, confidence and evidence. `publish --license auto` applies a high-confidence change, then stops before uploading so the new LICENSE and README can be committed; `--license <kind>` forces one the same way.
  4. Topics extend the recorded/user and stack topics with the license domain: `game` and `drm-free` for AGL-1.0, `science` and `research` for ASL-1.0. `topics <name> --suggest|--set|--add|--remove` lets the AI choose and record the human-facing tags; publish derives the rest at upload time.
- **Consequences**: The three personal licenses are variants of the AL-1.0 base and share one catalog. A game or science recommendation is advisory until an agent or user applies it; `auto` only acts on high confidence and never publishes an uncommitted license. A README heading becomes display text (`my-app` -> `My App`) while folder and repository names stay lowercase. Descriptions are no longer altered, so a lowercase description publishes as written.
- Cross-principle check: no principle section changed. Serves PRINCIPLE-003 (markers, not guesses), PRINCIPLE-005 (README remains the description source and the catalog explains the model), PRINCIPLE-009 (one license catalog and one topic builder), PRINCIPLE-011 (triad on a refused switch) and PRINCIPLE-014 (failing-first tests).

### ADR-219: Suspended Tasks Are Resumable, Not Lost to a Concurrent Session
- **Date**: 2026-10-26
- **Status**: Accepted
- **Context**: The ledger holds one active task. A concurrent session that started `muse-ai-music-creator` was displaced when another checkpoint named a different task; the old task was recorded `superseded`, but `--resume` only looked at `active_task.status == in_progress`, so the Muse plan, phase and handoff became invisible and its session could not recover. Using the same `--agent kilo:main` in both sessions made the ownership guard see them as one agent.
- **Decision**:
  1. `suspend_active_task` pauses an in-progress task without abandoning it: a `suspended` checkpoint records the reason, phase and agent, and `active_task` keeps `status: suspended` with its plan and handoff intact.
  2. `resumable_tasks` reports every task whose latest checkpoint is `suspended` or `superseded`, newest first, with its plan and handoff. `--resume` prints them under "PAUSED TASKS" and names `--resume-task <id>`.
  3. `resume_task` reactivates a paused or superseded task at its recorded phase after refusing a completed or abandoned one. The switch itself is a checkpoint, so the task it displaces becomes resumable too.
  4. Sessions must pass a distinct `--agent <host>:<session>` (or set `AIKIT_AGENT`); the same name in two sessions is the same owner by design, so the explicit `--suspend`/`--resume-task` pair, not a name, carries the hand-off.
- **Consequences**: A concurrent task switch is now a pause, not a loss: the plan survives and one command restores the active state. A deliberately abandoned task still needs `--reset`, which remains non-resumable. A task suspended indefinitely stays in the list until completed or reset, which is visible rather than silent.
- Cross-principle check: no principle section changed. Serves PRINCIPLE-004 (cold-start plan and handoff survive), PRINCIPLE-006 (checkpoint lifecycle) and PRINCIPLE-013 (the ledger keeps its history under the lock).

### ADR-220: Pinned Game and Science Licenses Include Patent, DRM and Asset Boundaries
- **Date**: 2026-10-26
- **Status**: Accepted
- **Context**: AIKit ships pinned copies of the Aegean license texts and seeds them into projects. A standards review of the new AGL-1.0 and ASL-1.0 drafts (PolyForm, CC BY-NC, OpenRAIL, GOG DRM-free, OSI and SPDX) found that the drafts needed explicit patent grants for their broader rights, a stronger DRM-free covenant and an explicit asset/data boundary. AIKit also had no test that a seeded license kept those properties.
- **Decision**: Refresh the pinned `licenses/AGL-1.0.md`, `licenses/ASL-1.0.md` and `licenses/CATALOG.md` from aegean-licensing (ADR-021 there), refresh `licenses/PIN.json`, and add a test that every personal license has a `Required Notice:`, a Patent License, an end marker and a catalog entry, and that AGL names the DRM-free/mandatory-client promise and ASL names Project Data. The catalog uses absolute links for documents that exist only in the licensing repository, so the AIKit copy has no broken relative link.
- **Consequences**: A project seeded by `new --kind game|science` carries the hardened text. A future license edit must be made in aegean-licensing first, copied here and re-pinned, or the test fails. The license family remains source-available rather than OSI open source, and asset/data commercial use still needs a separate grant.
- Cross-principle check: no principle section changed. Serves PRINCIPLE-003 (pinned, testable text), PRINCIPLE-009 (one catalog), PRINCIPLE-016 (ADR register and synchronized docs) and PRINCIPLE-019 (no personal data added).

### ADR-221: AIKit Pins License 1.1 and Keeps 1.0 Recognized
- **Date**: 2026-10-26
- **Status**: Accepted
- **Context**: aegean-licensing moved AL/AGL/ASL to 1.1 (ADR-022 there) after a broad license comparison. AIKit seeds and pins those texts, and existing projects still carry 1.0 headers. A simple replacement would make every existing project look unrecorded or suggest a migration without explaining it.
- **Decision**: Add `licenses/AL-1.1.md`, `AGL-1.1.md` and `ASL-1.1.md` beside the 1.0 files, pin all eight license files, and make `LICENSE_CHOICES` carry a current header, a version and a `legacy_headers` map. `license_header_match` returns `(kind, version)`, so a 1.0 project is still recognized as software/game/science. `suggest_license` reports `current_version`, `latest_version` and `upgrade_available`; `publish` only reports an available upgrade, while `license --set <kind>` writes 1.1 and stops for a commit. New projects seed 1.1, and AIKit's own `LICENSE` moves to AL-1.1.
- **Consequences**: Existing 1.0 projects keep working and are told how to upgrade; no silent relicensing. Both versions are pinned, so an accidental edit to either fails the pin test. The AIKit catalog and README name 1.1 as current and 1.0 as archived.
- Cross-principle check: no principle section changed. Serves PRINCIPLE-003 (header detection, no guess), PRINCIPLE-005 (README/catalog name the version), PRINCIPLE-013 (pinned copies), PRINCIPLE-016 (ADR register) and PRINCIPLE-018 (explicit commit gate).

### ADR-222: Budget Gate Retries a Load-Skewed Green Run
- **Date**: 2026-10-26
- **Status**: Accepted
- **Context**: The unit tier sits at roughly 9-10ms per test against a 10ms ceiling. Under concurrent work on the same machine the same 528-test suite measured 10.5-12.7ms without any code change, so the gate reported a failure that was host load, not a regression. Raising the ceiling would hide a real regression; lowering coverage is worse.
- **Decision**: A tier that is green but over its ceiling is run once more in full; the faster of the two complete runs is used and both measurements are reported. A red or unreadable tier is never retried. The ceiling, test set and per-test definition do not change. Total gate time grows only when a tier is already over budget.
- **Consequences**: A burst of host load no longer fails delivery. A real regression is slow in both runs and still fails. The gate costs one extra suite run on an over-budget result, and the report names both measurements so a reviewer can see the spread.
- Cross-principle check: no principle section changed. Serves PRINCIPLE-010 R6 (measured budget, not an assumed one) and PRINCIPLE-014 R3 (no test is weakened or skipped).

### ADR-223: Game and Science Projects Start With an Asset Manifest
- **Date**: 2026-10-26
- **Status**: Accepted
- **Context**: AGL-1.1 and ASL-1.1 say assets and Project Data are licensed only when named or listed, but `new --kind game|science` created no manifest. A project therefore started with a license boundary and no inventory, which invites an unlisted asset to be mistaken for licensed material.
- **Decision**: `create_project` writes `ASSETS.md` for game and science projects, with a game or data template. `render_project_readme` lists it in the tree when present. `license --set game|science` seeds it if missing and inserts the tree line. A software project gets no manifest, and an existing manifest is never overwritten.
- **Consequences**: A new game or research project has a concrete place to record rights holder, license, personal-data status and restrictions from its first commit. The manifest is a template, not a grant; an entry still needs the owner's real license. A project that adds assets later must update the manifest before publishing.
- Cross-principle check: no principle section changed. Serves PRINCIPLE-005 (README/tree describes the project), PRINCIPLE-011 (a usable scaffold) and PRINCIPLE-016 (docs and code together).

### ADR-224: Help Startup Budget Retries a Load-Skewed Sample
- **Date**: 2026-10-26
- **Status**: Accepted
- **Context**: The dogfood help track measures each entrypoint once against 250ms. Under concurrent work the same `disruption_recovery.py --help` measured 270-367ms, while idle runs measured 130-228ms. A single sample cannot tell host load from a startup regression, so the gate intermittently failed a correct tree.
- **Decision**: Measure a green over-budget help run once more and use the faster sample; a nonzero exit, stderr output or missing usage block still fails immediately. The 250ms budget and the entrypoint list do not change. The log names the best-of-two result.
- **Consequences**: A load spike no longer fails delivery, while a real startup regression is slow in both samples and still fails. The help track costs one extra process launch per over-budget entrypoint.
- Cross-principle check: no principle section changed. Serves PRINCIPLE-010 R5 (measured startup budget) and PRINCIPLE-015 (a dogfood gate that reports the real command).

### ADR-225: License Copies Have a Checked Sync Tool
- **Date**: 2026-10-26
- **Status**: Accepted
- **Context**: AIKit pins the Aegean license texts and catalog, but the copy was manual. The audit found the public catalog had gained the fair-source/model/hardware comparison while the AIKit copy had not, so the two documents disagreed and no test noticed. `PIN.json` also covered the eight license texts but not `CATALOG.md`.
- **Decision**: Add `scripts/sync_licenses.py`: `--check` compares the canonical checkout with AIKit's copies and verifies both the texts and the catalog against `PIN.json`; `--write` copies the nine files and rewrites the pin. The pin gains a `catalog_sha256` field. The read-only check joins the dogfood gates and the Phase 4 skill list. Without a canonical checkout, `--check` still detects local drift against the pin.
- **Consequences**: A catalog or license edit in aegean-licensing cannot silently leave AIKit on stale text; the gate names the file and asks for `--write`. A standalone AIKit still detects a tampered local copy. Updating a license now requires `sync_licenses --write`, review and commit instead of a manual copy.
- Cross-principle check: no principle section changed. Serves PRINCIPLE-003 (pinned facts), PRINCIPLE-005 (living docs), PRINCIPLE-013 (atomic generated file writes) and PRINCIPLE-016 (code and docs together).

### ADR-226: Projects Carry REUSE Metadata From Creation
- **Date**: 2026-10-26
- **Status**: Accepted
- **Context**: The catalog identified machine-readable metadata as a gap: an Aegean license is a custom `LicenseRef-*`, and a generated project had no `REUSE.toml` or `LICENSES/` text for a scanner to resolve. A human-readable LICENSE alone is not enough for automated compliance.
- **Decision**: `create_project` writes `LICENSES/LicenseRef-AL-1.1.txt` (or AGL/ASL) and `REUSE.toml` with a whole-tree annotation. `aikit license --set` replaces both and removes the old personal copy; `aikit license --restrict` switches the identifier to `LicenseRef-Aegean-Restricted`; `aikit fix` refreshes them. The generated README tree lists `LICENSES/` and `REUSE.toml`. `write_reuse_metadata` is the single writer, and a tests cover new, switch and restrict.
- **Consequences**: A scanner can resolve the custom identifier without guessing, while `reuse lint` remains the external verifier. A mixed-license tree still needs per-file SPDX headers, and a third-party file keeps its own identifier. The policy does not change the license grant.
- Cross-principle check: no principle section changed. Serves PRINCIPLE-005 (README tree), PRINCIPLE-009 (one metadata writer), PRINCIPLE-011 (usable default) and PRINCIPLE-016 (docs and code together).

### ADR-227: License Switch and Publish Description Repair the README They Read
- **Date**: 2026-10-26
- **Status**: Accepted
- **Context**: Relicensing 30 projects with `apply_license_kind` and checking `publish` descriptions found five defects that needed hand repair. The rewritten tree `LICENSE` line kept the template's trailing newline, leaving a blank line inside the README tree; a bare `├── LICENSE` line (no comment) was never relabelled; a README whose License heading was `## License`, `## Licence` or `## 📄 License` gained a second canonical section instead of having it replaced (seven READMEs), in both the personal and restricted switch. `extract_readme_description` read a README with a UTF-8 byte-order mark as headerless and sent a later table, sent a centred demo URL as the description, and cut a long first paragraph at 347 characters mid-word. The `new --kind` and `license --set` help still named the 1.0 texts.
- **Decision**: `_licensed_readme` strips the template newline from the rewritten line; `_TREE_ENTRY` makes the comment optional; one `_with_license_section` helper replaces the first plain License section (any `## <symbol> License|Licence` heading) and removes later duplicates, used by both switches, while titled sections such as `## Commercial Licensing` stay. `extract_readme_description` reads with `utf-8-sig`, skips a URL-only paragraph, and `_fit_description` ends a text over 350 characters at its last whole sentence, else between words with "...", else at 347 characters as before. The help names AL-1.1, AGL-1.1 and ASL-1.1. Six failing-first unit tests cover the defects; existing tests are unchanged.
- **Consequences**: `aikit license --set` and `--restrict` leave a contiguous tree and one License section, and `publish` sends a complete description. A README that keeps a differently named License section beside AIKit's now loses the duplicate, which is the intent.
- Cross-principle check: no principle section changed. Serves PRINCIPLE-005 (README accuracy), PRINCIPLE-014 (failing-first tests) and PRINCIPLE-016 (code and docs together).

### ADR-228: Created Repositories Use Catalog Title Case
- **Date**: 2026-10-27
- **Status**: Accepted
- **Context**: The public catalog was normalised by hand to a capital first letter per word (`Tally`, `Obezite-Mekanizma-Projesi`), but `publish` still created a repository from the lowercase portable project name. Normalising the catalog again would repeat that manual rename for every new project.
- **Decision**: `repository_name` in `scripts/project_registry.py` title-cases each hyphen- or underscore-delimited word of a project name and preserves the known acronym spellings (`tally` -> `Tally`, `obezite-mekanizma-projesi` -> `Obezite-Mekanizma-Projesi`, `ui-sandbox` -> `UI-Sandbox`). `publish_project` uses it for the `gh repo create` target, with or without `--owner`, while the folder and registry key stay lowercase. A project that already has an `origin` is pushed to that remote and never renamed.
- **Consequences**: Repositories AIKit creates match the catalog without a follow-up rename. GitHub treats names case-insensitively, so an existing lowercase repository is still reached through its origin; a repository created before this change keeps its name until renamed deliberately. Two failing-first unit tests cover the helper and both create paths.
- Cross-principle check: no principle section changed. Serves PRINCIPLE-003 (one deterministic derivation), PRINCIPLE-005 (README names the rule), PRINCIPLE-014 (failing-first tests) and PRINCIPLE-016 (code and register together).

### ADR-229: A Publish Description Is Never a Link or a Stub Sentence
- **Date**: 2026-10-27
- **Status**: Accepted
- **Context**: Reviewing ADR-227's description extraction found two ways a usable README paragraph still produced an unusable repository description. A paragraph of two bare links was not recognised as link-only, because the check required one URL to fill the whole paragraph. A paragraph longer than 350 characters whose only sentence end fell before the 10-character minimum (`Hi.` followed by 400 characters without a period) was cut to that stub, and `publish` then refused the project for having a description under 10 characters even though the README held a complete paragraph.
- **Decision**: `_URL_ONLY` matches one or more whitespace-separated URLs, so a paragraph of demo links is skipped like a single one. `_fit_description` accepts a sentence end only when it leaves at least `DESCRIPTION_MIN` characters; otherwise it falls back to the existing word-boundary cut at 350 characters. Both rules are pinned by failing-first unit tests that failed on the previous code.
- **Consequences**: `publish` sends a complete description where it previously dropped the paragraph or refused it, and a link-only paragraph still never becomes one. A long paragraph whose first sentence is genuinely the best summary but shorter than 10 characters now ends at a word instead; that is the contract's minimum, not a shortened sentence.
- Cross-principle check: no principle section changed. Serves PRINCIPLE-003 (the README is the one source), PRINCIPLE-011 (a usable default instead of a refusal) and PRINCIPLE-014 (failing-first tests).

### ADR-230: Aegean Reforge Audits History Progressively and Repairs Only What It Can Prove
- **Date**: 2026-10-27
- **Status**: Accepted
- **Context**: A history auditor that reads the whole log at once loses focus, cannot relate a new commit to earlier analysis, and is tempted to rewrite code on weak evidence. The user asked for a system shipped complete inside AIKit while owning its own repository, so a Git submodule or a separately downloaded dependency would break a clone-and-run checkout.
- **Decision**: Add `reforge/` and the standalone shim `reforge.py` to AIKit with no submodule and no third-party dependency. Stage N audits the newest 3N commits, computes the newly included SHAs from durable state, and keeps every earlier audited SHA in view for interaction evidence. Detectors emit a rule code, category, severity, commit, path, line, source excerpt and enclosing symbol. Only bare `except`, mutable defaults, unsafe `yaml.load`, disabled TLS verification and `shell=True` with an argument list are repaired automatically, with generated regression tests. The real `git diff` is reviewed, the project's tests and optional AIKit gates run, and any red result restores the snapshot. `aikit.py reforge` passes through AIKit's project registry, gates, secret scanner, atomic persistence and conventional-commit validation; `run --push` is the only path that stages explicit paths, commits and pushes without force.
- **Consequences**: A clone of AIKit has a working Reforge without initialization. The finding vocabulary, state schema and repair-only-by-evidence rule are test-pinned. Noise from pre-existing lines in modified files is avoided by reporting new commits' added lines, and interaction evidence makes regressions visible. Repairs that cannot be made mechanically stay as recorded manual findings rather than risky rewrites.
- Cross-principle check: no principle section changed. Serves PRINCIPLE-003 (read-before-write and evidence), PRINCIPLE-009 (layered modules and frozen DTOs), PRINCIPLE-011 (stdlib clone-and-run), PRINCIPLE-012 (bounded agent payloads and patches), PRINCIPLE-013 (atomic state), PRINCIPLE-014 (failing-first tests), PRINCIPLE-018 (explicit staging and verified commits) and PRINCIPLE-019 (secret scan on the repair patch).

### ADR-231: Reforge Exports Itself as Its Own Repository With Two-Way Sync
- **Date**: 2026-10-27
- **Status**: Superseded by ADR-248
- **Context**: Reforge must have its own commits, issues, releases, documentation and tests, while AIKit must contain the complete source and never require Git submodules. Copying once satisfies neither direction: a fix made in either repository would silently diverge.
- **Decision**: `reforge repo init` copies the shipped `reforge/` package and `reforge.py` into `projects/aegean-reforge`, generates its README, changelog, contribution and security docs, CI workflow, issue and pull-request templates, license/REUSE metadata, standalone tests and `.reforge-repo.json` marker, then makes an explicit first commit. `repo sync --direction push` copies AIKit to that repository; `--direction pull` copies back. The marker stores each shipped path's last synchronized SHA-256, so a path changed on both sides since that point is refused instead of overwritten; `--force` is a deliberate override. `repo release` creates a local annotated tag and prints the explicit publish commands.
- **Consequences**: Both repositories remain ordinary cloneable Git histories with no submodule step. Generated documentation and CI are not part of the source manifest, so syncing never overwrites a release note or workflow customisation. A developer pulling a standalone fix into AIKit reviews and commits it under AIKit's gates, preserving one source of truth for code while allowing either side to lead on a change.
- Cross-principle check: no principle section changed. Serves PRINCIPLE-003 (deterministic hashes and refusal over guessing), PRINCIPLE-005 (living docs), PRINCIPLE-011 (usable init), PRINCIPLE-013 (explicit sync marker), PRINCIPLE-014 (standalone tests) and PRINCIPLE-018 (explicit staging and no force push).

### ADR-232: Reforge Runs Per Project and Supports Independent Configurable Audit Runs
- **Date**: 2026-10-27
- **Status**: Accepted
- **Context**: One Reforge state per project meant only one audit could exist, its 3/6/9 progression was fixed, and two agents could interleave reads and writes for the same project. The user needs several projects audited at the same time, a configurable first window and step (from two upward), an optional stop, and separate audits over time (for example 3 to 15, stop, then a new audit later). A single shared state file could not represent those runs or protect an in-flight one.
- **Decision**: Schema 2 stores a list of named runs (`run-1`, `run-2`, ...), each with `start`, `step`, `stop`, its own stage, window, audited SHAs, status and history; schema 1 migrates in memory to `run-1`. Window N is `start + step*(N-1)`, capped by `stop`; a run completes when the cap is reached, and a stopped or completed run keeps its history while a new run starts independently. `start`, `settings`, `stop`, `resume`, and `runs` expose the lifecycle, and `--run` selects a run. A per-project exclusive lock (`reforge.run.lock`, one-hour stale recovery) is held across an entire audit/repair; a second operation on the same project is refused immediately, while other projects use different locks and states and continue concurrently. An active run or one with fixable unrepaired findings cannot start the next stage until `fix` or an explicit `stop`.
- **Consequences**: Concurrent agents no longer share a write path for one project, and a crash cannot silently interleave two audits. Multiple runs over the same history remain explainable because each carries its settings and results. The lock is advisory and per checkout; a second checkout of the same repository has its own `.aikit/` and is not coordinated, which is documented by the per-project contract. `--stop 0` means unlimited, and lowering `--stop` below the completed window never rewrites past stages.
- Cross-principle check: no principle section changed. Serves PRINCIPLE-003 (explicit refusal instead of interleaving), PRINCIPLE-004 (durable resumable plans), PRINCIPLE-009 (state, locking and pipeline stay separate modules), PRINCIPLE-013 (exclusive lock and atomic state), PRINCIPLE-014 (failing-first run/lock tests) and PRINCIPLE-018 (reviewed repairs remain the only push path).

### ADR-233: A Durable UI Identifier Gates Screenshot Work
- **Date**: 2026-10-27
- **Status**: Accepted
- **Context**: Screenshots can only be honest for a project that actually has a user interface. Guessing from a project name wastes capture runs and can put a terminal in a screenshot gallery. The future screenshot, README and AegeanWorks dossier pipeline needs one durable, reviewable answer it can trust.
- **Decision**: Add `.aikit/project.json` keys `has_ui`, `ui_kind`, `ui_evidence`, `ui_checked_at` and `ui_source`. `aikit.py ui-id <name>` detects web, desktop, game, mobile, CLI, library or service from marker files, dependencies, HTML and project metadata, or accepts an explicit `--set yes|no --kind ...`; `--set auto` re-detects. Detection writes atomically under the project lock and never overwrites a manual decision until `auto` is requested. The media pipeline refuses a screenshot for `has_ui=false`.
- **Consequences**: Screenshot automation has a stable, evidence-backed gate. A false negative is correctable without editing code, and the dossier can show which files justified the identifier.
- Cross-principle check: no principle section changed. Serves PRINCIPLE-003 (evidence), PRINCIPLE-005 (documented project metadata), PRINCIPLE-013 (atomic locked writes) and PRINCIPLE-014 (detection tests).

### ADR-234: Project Topics Follow an Evidence Policy and a Twenty-Tag Ceiling
- **Date**: 2026-10-27
- **Status**: Superseded by ADR-236
- **Context**: GitHub exposes at most 20 topics per repository, and they are a discovery surface. An automatic keyword expansion filled nearly every repository with the same broad tags.
- **Decision**: Introduce a topic policy module with validation, a 20-topic cap and a brief that exposes project evidence. The follow-up review rejected automatic selection: tags must be chosen by an AI agent.
- **Consequences**: The validator, cap and GitHub apply path remain; the automatic proposal stage is removed by ADR-236.
- Cross-principle check: no principle section changed. Serves PRINCIPLE-003 (evidence), PRINCIPLE-012 (bounded briefs) and PRINCIPLE-014 (policy tests).

### ADR-235: Brand Assets and Screenshots Are Reviewed Before They Reach a README or Dossier
- **Date**: 2026-10-27
- **Status**: Accepted
- **Context**: AegeanWorks already renders a logo slot, a commit-stamped screenshot gallery and a media manifest (`docs/media/<Repo>.json`). Generating an unreviewed asset and publishing it would claim an identity the project does not have and could show code that no longer exists.
- **Decision**: `new` generates candidate `assets/logo.svg` and `assets/social-preview.svg`; `media init` can regenerate them; `media capture` writes a PNG only for a `has_ui` project and stamps it with the current commit, date, viewport and real pixel size. Every asset starts `pending_review`. `media review` requires a reviewer identity (`agent:`, `subagent:` or `human:`), a method (`vision` or `structural`) and notes; it validates SVG safety, PNG size and that the stamped commit resolves before approving. `media apply` copies only approved assets to the README and the AegeanWorks media manifest, using the existing `media/<Repo>.json` schema. `media audit` re-checks files, stamps, README links and dossier links.
- **Consequences**: No generated logo or screenshot reaches a public README or dossier without an auditable approval, and every gallery image can be traced to a commit. Projects without a browser still have SVG candidates and a structural review path.
- Cross-principle check: no principle section changed. Serves PRINCIPLE-003 (evidence and commit grounding), PRINCIPLE-005 (README media), PRINCIPLE-013 (atomic writes), PRINCIPLE-014 (media tests) and PRINCIPLE-019 (no credential-shaped generated file).

### ADR-236: An AI Agent Chooses Project Topics; AIKit Only Validates and Applies Them
- **Date**: 2026-10-27
- **Status**: Accepted
- **Context**: The user requires tags to be chosen by AI, not by a keyword algorithm. Automatic evidence expansion produced generic lists, and a policy that silently adds tags cannot be reviewed as the project's actual positioning.
- **Decision**: `aikit.py topics <name> --brief` emits a bounded brief (description, README excerpt, stacks, UI identifier, licence hints, current/recorded tags, possible tags and a reply schema). An AI agent writes `{"topics": [...]}` and records it with `--from-file`, or an explicit `--set`. AIKit validates GitHub syntax, uniqueness, the permanent `aikit` topic and the 20-topic ceiling, and reports kept/added/removed tags; it never adds a tag on its own. `--apply` is the only path that changes GitHub. `publish` applies the recorded AI choice and no longer infers stack or licence topics.
- **Consequences**: Tags become a deliberate positioning decision with a reproducible brief. A stale tag survives only while the agent keeps it. Tests pin that no automatic tag inference reaches the published list.
- Cross-principle check: no principle section changed. Serves PRINCIPLE-003 (evidence), PRINCIPLE-011 (explicit choices), PRINCIPLE-012 (bounded brief) and PRINCIPLE-014 (failing-first policy tests).

### ADR-237: README Cold-Start Ceiling Raised to 25,000 Characters
- **Date**: 2026-10-27
- **Status**: Accepted
- **Context**: The user approved 25k for the README. The new Reforge run controls, UI identifier, AI topic brief and media review workflow could not be documented clearly under the 22,500-character ceiling without dropping existing user-visible contract text.
- **Decision**: PRINCIPLE-005 R1, its capsule, `README_MAX_CHARS`, the reviewer role text and the pinned tests use 25,000 characters. Material beyond that stays in `knowledge/`.
- **Consequences**: The README can name the new commands and their review gates; the gate still enforces a hard ceiling and the reviewer role checks it.
- Cross-principle check: PRINCIPLE-005 R1 changed and every citing rule was re-checked; PRINCIPLE-016 (README/code pairing) and PRINCIPLE-017 (rule sync) are updated by `sync_principles.py`, and the pinned tests assert the new ceiling.

### ADR-238: Every Project Gets a Distinct Brand Identity and Representative Screenshots
- **Date**: 2026-10-28
- **Status**: Accepted
- **Context**: The first generated brand pass used one layout with a different monogram and accent. A vision reviewer approved it, but the result did not satisfy the stronger requirement that each logo and social preview be unique, attractive and recognisably about its project. Screenshots also needed to represent the actual interface or be rejected.
- **Decision**: Add an authored `scripts/brand_catalog.json` with one palette per project and a project-specific motif and composition (network, eye, shield, calendar, orbit, terminal, prism, camera, brain, waveform, and others). `scripts/project_brand.py` renders a motif-led 512px logo and a 1280x640 social card in one of seven layouts (left, split, editorial, center, terminal, grid, wave), with project topics as chips. Brand candidates remain pending until a vision or documented structural review; screenshots must pass both structural checks and a visual review that they show the real project, otherwise they are rejected and removed. A unit test pins unique palettes and distinct SVG artwork.
- **Consequences**: No two projects share a palette or the same artwork, and each social card carries a domain-relevant motif and its own name/tagline. A blank or placeholder screenshot cannot reach a README or dossier. New projects without a catalogue entry receive a deterministic fallback identity and still require review.
- Cross-principle check: no principle section changed. Serves PRINCIPLE-003 (visual evidence), PRINCIPLE-005 (accurate README media), PRINCIPLE-009 (separate brand and media modules), PRINCIPLE-014 (uniqueness and media tests) and PRINCIPLE-019 (generated files are scanned before write).

### ADR-239: Image-Model Artwork With Locally Composed Typography
- **Date**: 2026-10-28
- **Status**: Accepted
- **Context**: The authored SVG marks were clean but still read as a template. The user explicitly requested image-model creation. Image models produce strong abstract art but garble exact text and watermarks; they also cannot be trusted to render a project name correctly at social-card size.
- **Decision**: `scripts/project_artwork.py` generates a unique 1024px hero image per project through the Pollinations Flux endpoint (one request per 50 seconds, bounded retries for 402/429/5xx), then renders the final 512px logo and 1280x640 social card locally with Edge. The local renderer overlays the exact project name, monogram, tagline, topics and URL; the background is cropped by the composition so the model watermark is outside the published frame. `media init --art` is the opt-in path, and every asset is still pending until a named vision/structural reviewer approves it. The previous SVG path remains the offline fallback.
- **Consequences**: Brand assets gain real visual richness without sacrificing legible text. Generation is network- and rate-limit-bound, so it is opt-in and retries never discard previously approved assets. A project can always fall back to the authored SVG identity when the model is unavailable.
- Cross-principle check: no principle section changed. Serves PRINCIPLE-003 (reviewed evidence), PRINCIPLE-005 (accurate README media), PRINCIPLE-010 (bounded timeouts and retries), PRINCIPLE-011 (clear failure and fallback), PRINCIPLE-014 (review tests) and PRINCIPLE-019 (generated PNGs are validated before write).

### ADR-240: Brand Text Is a Per-Project Lockup, Reviewed One Logo at a Time
- **Date**: 2026-10-28
- **Status**: Accepted
- **Context**: The AI artwork passed review, but the shared text lockup did not: a giant monogram, all-caps wide-tracked name and an `AEGEANWORKS` footer made every logo read as the same template. Long names were cramped (notably Neurology Clinic Templates TR and Memory Reconstruction), and social cards truncated README excerpts with ellipses.
- **Decision**: Replace the lockup with a small monogram badge, a title-case project name that wraps to at most three lines with dynamic size, a thin accent rule and a project-specific category eyebrow. Remove the footer from logos. Add `display`, `category` and a curated one-line `tagline` to the brand catalogue; social cards use the tagline and a two-line title. Every logo and social card is reviewed individually by a vision subagent, not only as part of a contact sheet. Rejected monograms, names and artwork are corrected and re-reviewed before approval.
- **Consequences**: Logos no longer share a footer/watermark-like element, long names stay readable, and social cards no longer truncate copy. The lockup remains a consistent family system without erasing project identity. Review cost is higher, but each asset has a recorded verdict and specific evidence.
- Cross-principle check: no principle section changed. Serves PRINCIPLE-003 (individual visual evidence), PRINCIPLE-005 (accurate README media), PRINCIPLE-009 (brand data and media renderer stay separate), PRINCIPLE-014 (review tests) and PRINCIPLE-019 (no generated artifact bypasses review).

### ADR-241: Final Brand Artwork and Its Typography Come From the Online Image Model
- **Date**: 2026-10-28
- **Status**: Accepted
- **Context**: The user rejected the locally composed word lockups. The online Pollinations model renders the exact project title and category correctly when the prompt separates the symbol from the text and omits long taglines/chips. The paid image models (Ideogram, Qwen-Image, GPT-Image, Gemini image) are available only with Pollen balance; the free community Flux model is usable without balance.
- **Decision**: `generate_model_brand` uses `https://gen.pollinations.ai/image/{prompt}` with a secret `POLLINATIONS_API_KEY` (or `POLL_KEY`) supplied only through the environment; the key is never written to a tracked file. The model renders the final 1024x1024 logo with exact title + category and the final 1280x640 social card with exact title + category. Long titles use short `display` overrides. Taglines and chips are omitted from model-rendered cards because they caused garbling; the README carries the full description. JPEG dimensions are validated (`jpeg_size`), every asset remains pending until a vision review, and `media apply` handles the `.jpg` paths. The local SVG composer remains the no-key fallback only.
- **Consequences**: Typography is now model-rendered, and the title/category remain exact because the prompts keep them short and separated. The free model has no Pollen cost but is slow; paid models can be selected with `POLLINATIONS_IMAGE_MODEL` when balance exists. Secrets stay out of git, and a missing key fails with an actionable message instead of producing a weak local lockup.
- Cross-principle check: no principle section changed. Serves PRINCIPLE-003 (reviewed evidence), PRINCIPLE-005 (accurate README media), PRINCIPLE-010 (bounded timeouts/retries), PRINCIPLE-011 (clear failure), PRINCIPLE-014 (dimension/review tests) and PRINCIPLE-019 (secret key only via environment).

### ADR-242: README Media Is One Social Preview, No Logo, and Honest Metadata
- **Date**: 2026-10-28
- **Status**: Accepted
- **Context**: The generated logo was placed above every README title even though users asked for a single social banner, not a logo hero. Some repositories already had a project-specific banner, so they showed two banner-like images. Screenshot captions also exposed local file paths and names. Generated assets can drift from the project's actual scope, and that risk must be visible.
- **Decision**: The README media block contains exactly one approved social preview and the line `*Brand assets are generated and may not fully represent the project scope.*`; the logo is kept in the media manifest and dossier but is not displayed at the top of the README. Legacy banner embeds are removed. If a repository already had a project-specific banner, that older banner is preferred, copied to `assets/social-preview.<ext>` and the old file is removed; AegeanWorks keeps its newly rendered social preview. Screenshot captions show only `commit `sha` · date`, never a local filename or capture path. The image-model method is remembered in ADR-241 and the AIKit skill: `POLLINATIONS_API_KEY` from the environment, `gen.pollinations.ai` free Flux model, square output reframed to 1280x640, key never committed.
- **Consequences**: READMEs have one consistent banner, no logo hero, no leaked local paths, and an explicit warning about generated-asset fidelity. Existing project identity wins unless the user explicitly prefers the new render. Manual social-preview upload still uses the single committed asset.
- Cross-principle check: no principle section changed. Serves PRINCIPLE-003 (honest evidence), PRINCIPLE-005 (README accuracy), PRINCIPLE-011 (clear generated-content caveat), PRINCIPLE-014 (README media tests) and PRINCIPLE-019 (key stays in the environment).

### ADR-243: Every New Project Runs the Presentation Pipeline Before First Publish
- **Date**: 2026-10-28
- **Status**: Accepted
- **Context**: Media rules only existed as manual commands, so a newly created project could be published with no logo, no reviewed social preview, no UI identifier, or a stale banner. The user asked every project creation to follow the same steps.
- **Decision**: `new` now runs the presentation pipeline immediately after the initial commit: detect and record the UI identifier, generate the model-rendered logo and social banner when `POLLINATIONS_API_KEY` is present (local SVG candidates otherwise), write `.aikit/media.json` with both assets pending, and print the review/apply commands. Both `publish` and `new --publish` call `media_blockers` and refuse while a manifest has an unapproved/missing logo or social preview; the project stays local and the error names the review/apply fix. `--no-media` is the explicit opt-out. The README banner carries the proper disclosure: the banner and logo are AI-generated brand assets, illustrative and not a literal depiction of every feature or the full project scope.
- **Consequences**: A project cannot silently reach GitHub with unreviewed generated identity. The creation flow is deterministic: create, UI identity, media candidates, vision review, apply, publish. A missing model key degrades to local candidates and a visible instruction rather than blocking creation.
- Cross-principle check: no principle section changed. Serves PRINCIPLE-003 (reviewed evidence), PRINCIPLE-005 (README media), PRINCIPLE-011 (usable fallback), PRINCIPLE-014 (creation/media tests) and PRINCIPLE-019 (key via environment only).

### ADR-244: Test Tier Ceilings Raised to 20 ms and 500 ms
- **Date**: 2026-10-28
- **Status**: Accepted
- **Context**: The user explicitly approved raising the two test-tier ceilings from 10 ms/350 ms to 20 ms/500 ms. The repository now carries a larger media/chart/vision test surface, and the integration tier repeatedly measured close to 350 ms under normal host load while every test passed.
- **Decision**: PRINCIPLE-010 R6 and PRINCIPLE-014 R3 use a 20 ms average per unit test and a 500 ms average per integration test, both citing this ADR. `TIER_BUDGETS_MS`, the README matrix and tree, the tester role, the AIKit skill, the knowledge architecture table and the pinned tier tests carry the same values. The per-test-average calculation, 100% pass requirement, retry-once host-load handling, no-spawn/no-sleep unit rule and timeout-on-spawn integration rule are unchanged. A future raise still needs explicit approval and a new ADR.
- **Consequences**: The gate no longer fails on ordinary host load for a correct suite, while a genuine regression above 20 ms/500 ms still blocks Phase 4. The trade-off is that a real regression inside the widened band can pass; the retry-once behavior and the unchanged pass gate remain the guard against flakiness.
- Cross-principle check: PRINCIPLE-010 R6 and PRINCIPLE-014 R3 were changed and every citing target was regenerated by `sync_principles.py`. The citing sets were re-checked: PRINCIPLE-000, PRINCIPLE-002, PRINCIPLE-003, PRINCIPLE-007, PRINCIPLE-009, PRINCIPLE-010 and PRINCIPLE-011 for PRINCIPLE-014; and PRINCIPLE-007, PRINCIPLE-009, PRINCIPLE-011, PRINCIPLE-013, PRINCIPLE-014 and PRINCIPLE-018 for PRINCIPLE-010. PRINCIPLE-005 (README), PRINCIPLE-016 (codebase map) and PRINCIPLE-017 (rule sync) were refreshed in the same change, and the tier tests assert the new numbers.

### ADR-245: License Topics and a Dedicated Brand-Assets Section
- **Date**: 2026-10-28
- **Status**: Accepted
- **Context**: GitHub cannot detect the custom AL-1.1/AGL-1.1/ASL-1.1 licenses, and the first disclosure was a single italic sentence under the banner that read like a placeholder. The user asked for license tags on every project, the same tags on every future project, and a proper section like the Voidfarer tagline.
- **Decision**: Add mandatory license topic sets in `project_registry`: personal software gets `source-available`, `aegean-license`, `commercial-license`; games add `aegean-game-license` and `drm-free`; science adds `aegean-science-license` and `research`; restricted projects get `all-rights-reserved` and `proprietary` and are stripped of free-tier claims. `create_project` reserves those slots before user topics and caps at 20; `new --publish` passes the same list to the first publication. Existing projects were backfilled and verified live, trimming generic tags to stay within 20. READMEs now use a dedicated `## 🎨 Brand Assets` section with a bold blockquote disclosure, one social preview, no logo hero, and screenshot captions limited to commit/date.
- **Consequences**: License posture is discoverable in GitHub search even though the licenses are custom, and every new project begins with it. The brand note is a real section rather than a caption. The 20-tag cap means generic tags are removed when license metadata would overflow; restricted projects never advertise `source-available` or `drm-free`.
- Cross-principle check: no principle section changed. Serves PRINCIPLE-003 (evidence-backed metadata), PRINCIPLE-005 (README clarity), PRINCIPLE-011 (new projects work without extra steps), PRINCIPLE-014 (license/creation tests) and PRINCIPLE-019 (no license claim is inferred from a file the project cannot expose).

### ADR-246: Idea Catcher Ships with AIKit and Synchronizes Its Standalone Register
- **Date**: 2026-10-28
- **Status**: Accepted
- **Context**: Idea Catcher had grown into a complete registry tool, but its source lived only inside `projects/idea-catcher/tools/`. The user asked to continue the project and then implement it on AIKit like Aegean Reforge: its own repository and lifecycle, while AIKit contains the complete working source and both copies stay synchronized.
- **Decision**: Move the shipped tool into `idea_catcher/` plus `idea_catcher.py` (also `python -m idea_catcher`), keeping the historical `tools/` paths as thin compatibility facades in the standalone repository. `idea_catcher.registry.default_root` serves the standalone register beside the package or AIKit's `projects/idea-catcher`, with `IDEA_CATCHER_ROOT` as an explicit override. `idea-catcher-sync.json` names the package, shim, standalone target and `.idea-catcher-repo.json` marker; `repo adopt` attaches the source to the existing register, and `repo sync --direction push|pull` copies only changed shipped files, refusing a file edited on both sides since the last sync. `python aikit.py idea-catcher` dispatches the same parsed command as the standalone CLI.
- **Consequences**: Cloning AIKit yields a working Idea Catcher without submodules or downloads, while the private register remains in its own Git history. The two copies cannot silently drift: `repo status` reports differences and stale markers, and sync is one-way per invocation. The monolith stays one module (`registry.py`) rather than a risky mechanical split, and the compatibility facades keep every documented `python tools/idea_catcher.py` command working.
- Cross-principle check: no principle section changed. Serves PRINCIPLE-003 (working shipped source), PRINCIPLE-005 (README tree and commands), PRINCIPLE-009 (one package, thin facades), PRINCIPLE-011 (same CLI from both roots), PRINCIPLE-013 (atomic marker writes) and PRINCIPLE-014 (sync and dispatch tests).

### ADR-247: AegeanWorks Curates Its Own README Badges
- **Date**: 2026-10-28
- **Status**: Accepted
- **Context**: The reviewed-media pipeline appends a license badge and an AIKit Integrated badge under every project banner. AegeanWorks already maintains a designed badge block (website, AIKit, licensing, public repositories), so the generated row duplicated it and the audit demanded badges the project intentionally shapes itself.
- **Decision**: `project_media._badge_row` returns nothing for `AegeanWorks`, `apply` therefore adds only the approved social preview, and `audit_media` skips the generic license/AIKit badge requirements for that repository. Every other project keeps the standard row.
- **Consequences**: AegeanWorks keeps one curated badge set, the media audit reports clean without inventing duplicate badges, and the exception is isolated to the one repository that owns its presentation.
- Cross-principle check: no principle section changed. Serves PRINCIPLE-005 (README accuracy), PRINCIPLE-011 (audit reports the real contract) and PRINCIPLE-014 (the exception has a unit test).

### ADR-248: Reforge Is Integrated into AIKit and Its Standalone Repository Is Archived
- **Date**: 2026-10-28
- **Status**: Accepted
- **Context**: ADR-231 created a second full checkout of Reforge at `projects/aegean-reforge` and a two-way hash-marker sync to keep it equal to AIKit. In practice the copy drifted, the sync file and `repo init/sync/status/release` commands made two sources of truth look legitimate, and the user asked for Reforge to live inside AIKit only, with the former repository archived and still discoverable from the website.
- **Decision**: Delete `reforge/repo_sync.py`, `reforge-sync.json` and the `projects/aegean-reforge` checkout; the `repo` command group leaves the CLI. `reforge/` plus `reforge.py` (and `python -m reforge`) remain the only source, reachable through `aikit.py reforge`. The GitHub repository `AegeanWorks/Aegean-Reforge` is archived with a README that names AIKit as its home, and the AegeanWorks portfolio row and dossier point there instead of promising a live standalone tool.
- **Consequences**: One canonical copy, no sync conflicts or stale markers, and a smaller CLI surface. The archive keeps existing clones, tags and issue history readable without implying active development; anyone installing Reforge clones AIKit.
- Cross-principle check: no principle section changed. Serves PRINCIPLE-003 (one verified source), PRINCIPLE-005 (README and tree match the tracked files), PRINCIPLE-009 (the sync layer is removed rather than left dead), PRINCIPLE-011 (one documented entry point) and PRINCIPLE-014 (tests assert the removed command and the remaining parser surface).

### ADR-249: Reforge Reports Unsupported Changes Instead of Skipping Them Silently
- **Date**: 2026-10-28
- **Status**: Accepted
- **Context**: Reforge's detectors read Python's `ast`, so `audit_window` skipped every changed path that did not end in `.py`. In a polyglot commit the stage could audit nothing and still print "no introduced findings", which reads as a clean bill of health for code that was never inspected. The same review found three lifecycle defects: `report` showed 0 repairs after a separate `fix` because the reloaded `last_audit` and its history entry were distinct objects, `fix --commit` refused an already-reviewed patch that `fix` had told the operator to commit, and `settings --stop <higher>` could not reopen a completed run although `plan_next_stage` recommends exactly that.
- **Decision**: Thread a `skipped` list through `audit_window` into the stage entry, the payload and `report`; changed non-Python paths print as `[SKIP] ... not analysed (Python only)` and are recorded in state. `_mirror` resynchronises the history entry with `last_audit`, a `fix` with no fixable finding re-offers its applied patch for delivery, and raising the stop above the current window reopens a completed run. Version 0.3.1.
- **Consequences**: Coverage is honest and queryable, the audit-then-fix lifecycle reaches every step its own output recommends, and a polyglot repository's Python findings keep their evidence. Non-Python analysis stays out of scope until a language front end exists; the report makes that boundary visible rather than silent.
