- Cross-principle check: no principle section changed. Serves PRINCIPLE-003 (claims match inspected code), PRINCIPLE-011 (every state has a documented next step), PRINCIPLE-013 (state and history stay one consistent record) and PRINCIPLE-014 (regression tests cover each fix).

### ADR-250: Reforge Mirrors Its Runs into the Repository's Task Ledger
- **Date**: 2026-10-28
- **Status**: Accepted
- **Context**: Reforge kept its whole record in `.aikit/reforge.json`, which no cold-start path reads. An audit interrupted mid-run was invisible to `disruption_recovery.py --resume`, so a fresh agent could start unrelated work on top of an uncommitted repair patch and a stopped run could not be found again.
- **Decision**: `reforge/ledger.py` appends one checkpoint per lifecycle event (start, each stage, stop, resume) to the ledger the audited repository already has, under task id `reforge:<run-id>`. Entries are written through AIKit's `locked_ledger` and appended to `checkpoints` only: calling `record_checkpoint` would supersede whatever task the operator is working on, so `active_task` is never touched. A stopped run is `suspended`, a run reaching its stop is `completed`, and a stage is `in_progress`, which makes `resumable_tasks` surface exactly the runs that can still be resumed. Because the ledger is a tracked file, the clean-tree check and the patch review treat the ledger's `.bak` and `.lock` siblings as Reforge bookkeeping; the ledger itself is never committed by Reforge. A repository without a ledger is audited unchanged. Text/question answers remain untrusted: no ledger content feeds an audit decision.
- **Consequences**: Cold start now sees an open audit, `--resume` lists a stopped Reforge run beside the operator's paused task, and the operator's active task is preserved. Two records (`.aikit/reforge.json` and the ledger) must agree; the state file stays authoritative and the ledger is a mirror, so a lost ledger entry never changes an audit. Repositories outside AIKit keep working with no new requirement.
- Cross-principle check: no principle section changed. Serves PRINCIPLE-002 (lifecycle ordering is visible in the ledger), PRINCIPLE-004 (cold-start resumption finds in-flight work), PRINCIPLE-006 (checkpoints name the agent and survive compaction), PRINCIPLE-013 (ledger writes stay inside its lock and atomic writer) and PRINCIPLE-019 (ledger entries carry paths and counts, never secrets).

### ADR-251: README Cold-Start Ceiling Raised to 30,000 Characters
- **Date**: 2026-10-28
- **Status**: Accepted
- **Context**: ADR-237 set the ceiling at 25,000 characters. AIKit now documents a project cockpit, a 20-principle table, Reforge's windows and repairs, Idea Catcher's register, and the media pipeline in that one cold-start file; at 25,000 the four project commands had to be compressed to the point where their flags were no longer explainable, and a documentation change kept failing the gate it was meant to satisfy.
- **Decision**: PRINCIPLE-005 R1, its Tier 1 capsule, `README_MAX_CHARS`, the reviewer role text and the pinned tests use 30,000 characters. Material that still does not fit stays in `knowledge/`.
- **Consequences**: 5,000 more characters buy back the command explanations an agent needs on its first turn. The file stays a cold-start summary rather than a manual, and ADR-237 remains the record of why the earlier number was chosen.
- Cross-principle check: PRINCIPLE-005 is cited by PRINCIPLE-011, PRINCIPLE-016 and PRINCIPLE-017. PRINCIPLE-011 (zero-friction onboarding) needs the commands and prerequisites on the first screen, which the extra room restores. PRINCIPLE-016 (knowledge synchronization) still points anything that does not fit into `knowledge/`, so the README does not absorb the knowledge base. PRINCIPLE-017 (living principles) keeps the number stated once in `GUIDELINES.md` and checked by the gate, so the ceiling cannot drift from the code.

### ADR-252: Idea Catcher Is Integrated into AIKit and Keeps Its Register Private
- **Date**: 2026-10-28
- **Status**: Accepted
- **Context**: ADR-246 created a second full checkout at `projects/idea-catcher` and a one-way hash-marker sync to keep it equal to AIKit. The standalone repository has no GitHub remote, so it could not become a discoverable public repository, and the duplicate register made two layouts the tool had to recognize. The user asked for the tool to live inside AIKit only, for the ideas it collected to stay invisible to the public, for the local repository to be archived, and for `projects/idea-catcher` to be removed.
- **Decision**: Delete `idea_catcher/repo_sync.py`, `idea-catcher-sync.json`, the `repo init/adopt/sync/status` command group and the sync tests; `idea_catcher/` plus `idea_catcher.py` (and `python -m idea_catcher`) remain the only source, reachable through `aikit.py idea-catcher`. The register data (`targets.json`, `ideas.json`, `reviews.json`, `MATRIX.md`, `CONCEPTS.md`, `dossiers/`, `intake/` and the project's `.aikit/` memory) moves to `.idea-catcher/` at the AIKit root, which `.gitignore` excludes, and `registry.default_root()` resolves there after the standalone package-beside-data layout. The whole former repository, including `.git`, is archived as a zip on the operator's Desktop before `projects/idea-catcher` is deleted. Version 1.1.0.
- **Consequences**: One canonical copy, no sync conflicts or stale markers, and a smaller CLI surface. A clone of AIKit still gets the working tool but no data, so the register can only be read on the machine that owns it; the archive keeps the complete history and every collected idea readable offline. `IDEA_CATCHER_ROOT` remains the explicit way to point at another register.
- Cross-principle check: no principle section changed. Serves PRINCIPLE-005 (the README documents the new layout and private-data rule), PRINCIPLE-009 (the tool keeps one module boundary without a sync adapter), PRINCIPLE-016 (architecture, services and decisions name the new home), and PRINCIPLE-019 (the git-ignore baseline protects the private register and no idea content enters a commit).

### ADR-253: Reforge Reviews Every Changed Text File and Finds the Ledger by Name
- **Date**: 2026-10-29
- **Status**: Accepted
- **Context**: A run over AIKit's own history reported "no introduced findings" while skipping all 33 changed files as "Python only", and printed "no AIKit task ledger in this repository" although `.agents/task_ledger.json` existed. Reforge is the tool a session uses to review the changes it makes while following the lifecycle, but on a repository whose commits are mostly documentation and configuration it reviewed nothing, and its ledger mirror never fired because `workspace_ledger_path` answers `.aikit/task_ledger.json` whenever the `.aikit/` folder exists — which Reforge itself creates for its state. The same folder then made AIKit's own gates treat the checkout as a project: `update_knowledge_base.py --check` looked for `.aikit/codebase_map.md` and failed, so the dogfood gate went red.
- **Decision**: `reforge/textdetect.py` reviews every changed text file that is not Python: JSON and TOML must parse with the standard library, a Markdown code fence must close, and a committed merge-conflict marker is a defect. `audit_window` sends `.py` to the AST detectors and every other readable text revision to these checks; only a revision containing a NUL byte is reported as not analysed, and the stage records and prints a `reviewed` file count. `reforge/ledger.py` resolves the ledger by asking for each known location (`.aikit/task_ledger.json`, `.agents/task_ledger.json`) and takes the one that exists, instead of trusting the folder that Reforge's own state happens to create. Project memory is identified by its `.aikit/project.json` marker (or a `.aikit/` folder with no `.agents/` sibling), so a tool state folder can no longer reclassify AIKit in `workspace_ledger_path` and `memory_dir`. Version 0.6.0.
- **Consequences**: A documentation or configuration commit now gets a real review, and the coverage line names what was inspected instead of listing everything as skipped. The text rules are deliberately structural — parse errors and markers a diff can prove — so no Markdown style opinion or guessed link check enters the report, and a binary asset stays honestly labelled. Repositories with either ledger layout record their audits again, and AIKit keeps its own ledger and knowledge base whatever a tool writes under `.aikit/`. A project whose `.aikit/` lost `project.json` while also carrying a copied `.agents/` would be read as AIKit until `aikit fix` restores the marker.
- Cross-principle check: no principle section changed. Serves PRINCIPLE-003 (findings cite inspected content), PRINCIPLE-014 (regression tests cover both fixes), PRINCIPLE-016 (architecture, services and the README describe the coverage), PRINCIPLE-002 (audits are visible to a cold start through the ledger), and PRINCIPLE-009 (the text review is its own single-role module).

### ADR-254: Reforge Reviews Changes with a Read-Only AI Agent and Applies Only Validated Fixes
- **Date**: 2026-10-29
- **Status**: Accepted
- **Context**: Reforge's audit is the step that reviews the changes a session just made, but its evidence was purely mechanical: an AST rule, a parse check or a conflict marker. A wrong condition, a missing guard, or a comment that no longer matches the code passed the audit untouched. The user asked for the review to happen through AI and for the fixes to follow, and for several projects to be auditable at the same time without sharing locks or state.
- **Decision**: Add `reforge/ai_review.py`. After the deterministic audit, it builds a bounded brief from the stage's own diff (12 files, 6,000 characters per file, 14,000 total) and asks the read-only `architect` sub-agent for one JSON object of findings on a free model (`opencode/deepseek-v4.1-flash-free` by default, `--ai-model` overrides). The sub-agent runs from the AIKit checkout, where its profile exists, and receives the diff as its whole input. Every answer is untrusted: a finding is accepted only when its path was changed by the stage and its line exists; a suggested `old`/`new` replacement must occur exactly once and leave a Python file parsable, otherwise the finding is kept for manual review without the patch. Accepted patches are applied by `reforge.repair.apply_text_patches` inside the existing snapshot, test, gate and diff-review transaction, so a failure rolls the whole stage back. `audit` and `run` enable the review by default, `--no-ai` disables it, a missing OpenCode or model is reported as skipped, and the result records status, model, seconds and rejected count. Separately, the lock path and the dispatch target are resolved before locking, so two spellings of one checkout share one lock while distinct projects keep independent locks and state. Version 0.7.0.
- **Consequences**: A stage now gets a semantic review with a line of evidence, and a confident suggestion can be applied, tested and reverted like a mechanical repair. The AI is contained: it cannot edit, commit or write a ledger, it cannot name a file outside the stage, and its patch never bypasses the diff review. A slow or unavailable model costs a skipped review, not a failed audit; the free-model rule keeps the feature at zero cost. Parallel projects stay independent, and the added state keys are additive for readers of schema 2.
- Cross-principle check: no principle section changed. Serves PRINCIPLE-003 (untrusted answers are grounded in the inspected diff), PRINCIPLE-009 (the reviewer is one module with a single role and the run lock stays per project), PRINCIPLE-011 (missing prerequisites are a reported skip, never a traceback), PRINCIPLE-012 (bounded brief, static prefix first, schema-checked reply, free model only, timeout), PRINCIPLE-014 (regression tests cover validation, rejection, application and concurrent projects), and PRINCIPLE-019 (credential-shaped content in a patch still hits the existing diff scan).

### ADR-255: The AI Brief Is Redacted and a Patch Must Overlap the Reviewed Hunk
- **Date**: 2026-10-29
- **Status**: Accepted
- **Context**: The first Reforge run over AIKit audited the commits that introduced the AI review and reported three real defects in it. The brief sent the stage diff to a third-party free model unredacted, although the audited history is exactly where a leaked credential lives. A suggested replacement was validated against the whole file, not the reviewed hunk, so a prompt injected into audited text could steer an edit into any uniquely quotable line of a staged file. The README also claimed `--commit` stages `.aikit/reforge.json` while AIKit's root now ignores that folder, so the documented behaviour and the ignore rule disagreed.
- **Decision**: `build_brief` runs every diff through the project's `scripts.check_secrets.scrub_text` before the text is included, records the redaction count, and the CLI prints it; the prompt states that the diff is the complete input. `findings_from_payload` receives the line numbers the stage added per path and `_patch_problem` refuses a replacement whose quoted text does not overlap them. The README separates a project's committed state from AIKit's git-ignored local `.aikit/` folder. Version 0.7.1.
- **Consequences**: No recognised credential shape leaves the machine through the review, and a model can only suggest an edit inside the change under review. A secret the scanner does not recognise still reaches the model when AI review is enabled, so `--no-ai` remains the answer for an untrusted network. The fourth finding (a dangling ADR reference) was rejected: ADR-253 and ADR-254 both exist in `knowledge/decisions.md` and arrived in the window the audit read.
- Cross-principle check: no principle section changed. Serves PRINCIPLE-019 (redaction happens at the boundary, not by model goodwill), PRINCIPLE-003 (a patch is grounded in the inspected lines), PRINCIPLE-012 (the redaction and scope check cost no extra model call), and PRINCIPLE-005 (the README no longer contradicts the ignore rule).

### ADR-256: AI Evidence Must Quote a Line That Exists in the File
- **Date**: 2026-10-29
- **Status**: Accepted
- **Context**: The second and third Reforge runs over AIKit kept reporting documentation defects that had already been fixed, citing the old wording as though it were current. The brief is a unified diff, and the model repeatedly read a `-` line (removed by the commit) as the file's present content. Every such finding is work that does not exist, and it blocks a run because a run may not advance past pending findings.
- **Decision**: The prompt now states the diff semantics explicitly: a `+` line exists after the commit, a `-` line was removed and is not in the file, and a leading space is unchanged context. Independently of the model, `findings_from_payload` verifies the finding's `evidence` against the current file's stripped lines and drops it with a recorded reason when the quote is absent. Version 0.7.2.
- **Consequences**: A stale finding produced from a removed line can no longer reach the report, so an AI review describes the tree as it is. A legitimate finding still needs a verbatim quote, which the prompt asks for; a paraphrase is treated as unverifiable and dropped rather than trusted. The guard is deterministic and costs one file read per finding.
- Cross-principle check: no principle section changed. Serves PRINCIPLE-003 (a claim is grounded in content that exists), PRINCIPLE-014 (a regression test covers the removed-line case), PRINCIPLE-011 (the drop reason is reported instead of a traceback), and PRINCIPLE-012 (no extra model call).

### ADR-257: The AI Review Is Told Which Files It Cannot See
- **Date**: 2026-10-29
- **Status**: Accepted
- **Context**: The fourth run over AIKit reported that the redaction and patch-scope guarantees were unimplemented, although both were in the code. The brief is bounded to twelve files and 14,000 characters, so the catch-up documentation was included while the Python that implements it was not, and the model inferred absence of code from absence of a diff. A reviewer that guesses about files it was never shown produces confident wrong findings.
- **Decision**: The prompt states that a file absent from the diff is unknown and forbids claiming that a feature, field or check is unimplemented outside the lines shown, telling the model to stay silent instead. The brief lists every changed path and names the ones omitted from the diff, so the boundary is explicit rather than implied. Version 0.7.3.
- **Consequences**: Findings describe only what the given lines support. A truncated review may miss a real defect in an omitted file, which is honest: the stage reports truncation, and a narrower window reviews fewer files. The change costs no extra model call.
- Cross-principle check: no principle section changed. Serves PRINCIPLE-003 (no claim beyond the evidence), PRINCIPLE-012 (the brief stays inside its budget and says so), PRINCIPLE-014 (the brief test asserts the guard), and PRINCIPLE-011 (truncation is reported, not hidden).

### ADR-258: AI Review Fails Closed Without the Secret Scanner
- **Date**: 2026-10-29
- **Status**: Accepted
- **Context**: The fifth Reforge run found that the redaction step degraded silently when `scripts.check_secrets` could not be imported: the raw diff was still sent to the third-party model and the reported redaction count stayed zero, which reads as "nothing to redact". A guarantee that disappears with a missing import is not a guarantee.
- **Decision**: `scrubber_available()` gates the review. When the scanner cannot be loaded, `review_stage` returns `skipped` with an explicit reason and never calls the model; no diff leaves the machine unredacted. The README states this. Version 0.7.4.
- **Consequences**: A standalone Reforge checkout without AIKit's scanner loses the AI review instead of leaking history, which is the safer failure. The deterministic audit is unaffected, so the tool still works.
- Cross-principle check: no principle section changed. Serves PRINCIPLE-019 (no egress without redaction), PRINCIPLE-011 (a clear skip reason, no traceback), and PRINCIPLE-014 (a test proves the model is never called).

### ADR-259: The AI Review Reads the Stage's Net Diff, Whole Files Only
- **Date**: 2026-10-29
- **Status**: Accepted
- **Context**: Six Reforge runs over AIKit produced repeated false positives about its own AI feature. Two defects in the brief caused them. First, a stage spans several commits, but `_changed_paths` de-duplicated by path while walking the commits, so a file changed more than once was shown only through the oldest commit's diff, which later commits had already replaced. Second, every diff was cut at 6,000 characters and the first hunks were kept, so the model saw a file ending in `[truncated]` and inferred that code beyond the cut did not exist. Both defects made the reviewer confidently wrong about code it had not seen.
- **Decision**: `reforge/git_history.py` gains `changed_files_range`, `added_lines_range` and `range_diff`, and the brief now takes one net diff per path from the commit before the stage to its last commit, so every file appears once in the state the stage leaves behind. A file is included whole or not at all: a diff larger than the per-file budget is omitted and named, never cut. The budgets grow to 10 files, 8,000 characters each and 24,000 in total. `findings_from_payload` receives the omitted paths and drops any finding whose message or evidence reasons about one of them. Version 0.8.0.
- **Consequences**: The reviewer now sees the change it is judging, as the tree ends up, so a fixed line cannot come back as a live defect. A very large stage reviews fewer files and says which, and the deterministic audit still covers all of them. Patch validation is computed against the same accumulated range, so a suggested replacement must still land inside what the stage added.
- Cross-principle check: no principle section changed. Serves PRINCIPLE-003 (claims are grounded in the net change), PRINCIPLE-012 (bounded input, no wasted half-hunks), PRINCIPLE-014 (unit and integration tests pin both defects), and PRINCIPLE-016 (architecture, services and decisions describe the range-based review).

### ADR-260: Reforge Honours the Project's Documented Suppression Marker
- **Date**: 2026-10-29
- **Status**: Accepted
- **Context**: Deeper auditing of AIKit's history produced fifteen `swallowed-exception` findings in one stage, and every one of them was already a reviewed decision: the code carries AIKit's own `hygiene: allow <reason>` marker, which the project hygiene scan accepts on the finding's line or the line directly above. Reforge knew nothing about that convention, so it reported each deliberate best-effort cleanup as a live defect and buried the run in noise.
- **Decision**: `reforge/detectors.py` gains `allow_marker`, the same one-line-or-above rule AIKit's scanner uses (ADR-147), and `detect_source` drops a finding whose line is marked. `reforge/textdetect.py` applies the same filter to conflict markers, invalid JSON/TOML and unclosed fences. The marker cannot suppress a syntax error or a deleted line, and two lines of distance is not enough. Version 0.8.1.
- **Consequences**: A repository that documented why a handler is deliberately silent is audited as intended, and the remaining findings are the ones worth a human's time. The marker is the project's own text, so Reforge inherits a convention instead of inventing one, and a repository with no markers behaves exactly as before.
- Cross-principle check: no principle section changed. Serves PRINCIPLE-003 (a marked decision is evidence, not a defect), PRINCIPLE-014 (tests pin the accepted and rejected distances), PRINCIPLE-009 (one shared helper, no duplicated rule), and PRINCIPLE-012 (fewer false findings, fewer wasted tokens).

### ADR-261: A Review Brief Is Sized to the Model, Not Capped at 16,000 Characters
- **Date**: 2026-10-29
- **Status**: Accepted
- **Context**: Reforge's AI review must analyse every change in a stage: defects, gaps, missing cases and better implementations. The first implementation obeyed PRINCIPLE-012 R1 literally, so each request was capped at 16,000 characters and larger stages were spread over several requests, or earlier versions dropped files. The operator asked for a full review of every change and noted the reviewer's window is far larger. The cap was never the model's limit: the `architect` profile runs `gemini-3.8-flash` at 1,048,576 tokens, and even the free 128k models hold roughly 270,000 characters at 70% usable.
- **Decision**: PRINCIPLE-012 R1 gains one documented exception: a code review brief may size itself to the selected model's documented context window in `knowledge/model_benchmarks.md` (R8), at no more than 70% of it, and must split into further requests, never dropping any part of the change, when the change would exceed it. `reforge/ai_review.py` implements it: `brief_budget(model)` maps each documented model to `tokens * 3 * 0.7` characters, `build_briefs` sends the whole stage in one request when it fits and otherwise splits by hunk and by line so coverage stays complete, and `--ai-budget` overrides it explicitly. An unrecognised model keeps the 16,000-character default, so the conservative path is the fallback. Version 0.9.0.
- **Consequences**: A normal stage is one request and is reviewed whole, which is what "analyse all changes" requires; a genuinely huge stage is chunked and still complete, with the request count printed. The rule now describes what the code does, so a reader cannot find the two in conflict. Cost and latency grow with the stage instead of being capped, which is the operator's stated preference for review work.
- Cross-principle check: every citing principle. PRINCIPLE-000 R6 (the amendment is narrower than the rule and states its bound, so nothing silently overrides another rule); PRINCIPLE-012 (R1 amended by this ADR, bounded by R2's byte-identical static prefix, R4's schema-checked reply, R6's `MAX_REQUESTS` cap and R8's model-window figures); PRINCIPLE-004 R3/R5 (the brief is still line slices, never whole-file concatenation, and the agent's own output stays short); PRINCIPLE-006 R5 (a stage still records a checkpoint before stopping); PRINCIPLE-008 R1/R6 (the brief is a bounded, role-scoped sub-agent task); PRINCIPLE-009 R6 (the model choice is grounded in the benchmark table); PRINCIPLE-011 R6 (an unavailable model is a reported skip with a Fix, never a traceback); PRINCIPLE-015 (dogfooding still covers the CLI paths); and PRINCIPLE-019 R4/R5 (the brief is redacted fail-closed before it leaves the machine). PRINCIPLE-003 keeps every finding grounded in the change, and PRINCIPLE-014 pins each behaviour with a test.

### ADR-262: Reforge Plans the Review, the AI Performs It, and the Rules Are Opt-In
- **Date**: 2026-10-29
- **Status**: Accepted
- **Context**: Reforge exists to automate one recurring request: "look at the changes since the last N commits, find bugs, fixes, gaps, improvements and further implementations, do what is necessary, then push" — repeated with N = 3, then 6, then 9. That is a review of arbitrary changes in any language, not a language-specific rule sweep. Building it around AST detectors made Reforge a second linter: on documentation-heavy stages it found nothing, and on code it reported only the twenty-odd mechanical patterns it knew, never an improvement or a missing implementation. Meanwhile the honest failure it did surface was its own: a brief larger than Windows' 32,767-character command line was passed as an argv element and the sub-agent could not start (WinError 206).
- **Decision**: Reforge becomes the planner and bookkeeper. It selects the window, records the stage, tracks findings, repairs and delivery, and owns the ledger, locks and state. The review is the AI agent's: every changed hunk is sent with instructions to report defects and concrete improvements, in any language. The rule pre-pass becomes opt-in (`--rules`), kept for a machine with no model and for the five mechanically repairable rules. A brief longer than `ARGV_SAFE_CHARS` (8,000) is written to a temporary attachment and passed with `--file`, so no command-line limit can block a review; the attachment is deleted afterwards. AI findings carry `category: improvement` for `kind: improvement`. Version 0.9.1.
- **Consequences**: The tool answers the request it was built for instead of duplicating a linter, and its findings can now be improvements rather than only defects. A repository with no reachable model loses the AI review but keeps the planner and the opt-in rules. The argv fix removes a hard platform failure that only appeared once briefs grew to the model's real window.
- Cross-principle check: no principle section changed. Serves PRINCIPLE-003 (every finding still cites path, line and commit), PRINCIPLE-009 (one planner role, one review module, no duplication of the linter), PRINCIPLE-011 (a missing model is a reported skip with a Fix), PRINCIPLE-012 (the brief is bounded by the model window and the static prefix stays byte-identical), PRINCIPLE-014 (tests cover the argv attachment, the opt-in rules and the improvement category), and PRINCIPLE-019 (the brief is redacted fail-closed before it leaves).

### ADR-263: Reforge Leaves the Review to the Host Agent
- **Date**: 2026-10-29
- **Status**: Accepted; supersedes ADR-254..262
- **Context**: The AI review subsystem kept growing around a task the driving agent already performs: model-sized briefs, argv attachments for Windows, request caps, redaction, omitted-file reasoning, evidence rules and a read-only subagent profile. Every fix addressed a failure of the orchestration itself, not of the audit. The operator asked for Reforge to plan the history review and let the host agent analyze the packet directly, with no subagents and no model call in the tool.
- **Decision**: `reforge/review.py` is the only review module. `audit` and `run` write the stage's complete net diff, from the commit before the stage to its last commit, every changed text file once, with binary paths named and credential-shaped spans redacted, to `.aikit/reforge.reviews/stage-<n>-<sha>.md`. The packet carries the JSON answer schema. The host agent returns a findings file; `fix --findings FILE` (or `run --findings FILE`) validates every path, line, evidence quote, exact-once replacement, stage overlap and Python parse, applies only what survives, and leaves the normal snapshot, tests, gates and diff-review transaction in charge. `run --rules` remains the deterministic rule-only path. `reforge/ai_review.py` and the `--no-ai`, `--ai-model`, `--ai-timeout` and `--ai-budget` options are deleted; the state key `ai` becomes `review`, and a legacy `ai` entry is still readable. Version 1.0.0.
- **Consequences**: Reforge no longer opens a network connection, needs a key, spawns a subagent or reports a model as skipped; an agent session has one explicit packet/findings contract instead of two analysis paths. The cost is that a repository with no agent driving it gets no semantic review, only the opt-in rules and the packet for a person to read. Historical ADRs 254..262 stay in the register as the record of the removed path.
- Cross-principle check: no principle section changed. Serves PRINCIPLE-003 (every accepted finding is grounded in the stage diff and the current file), PRINCIPLE-009 (one review role, one review module), PRINCIPLE-011 (no model, key or subagent is required), PRINCIPLE-012 (the packet is written once; the host controls its own review budget), PRINCIPLE-014 (unit and integration tests pin packet construction and answer validation), and PRINCIPLE-019 (credential spans are redacted before the packet is written).

### ADR-264: Rejected and Duplicate Ideas Leave the Default Target Listing
- **Date**: 2026-10-29
- **Status**: Accepted
- **Context**: The private register carries ideas that are rejected as harmful and ideas that duplicate a shipped capability or a stronger idea. Keeping either kind in every target listing wastes the next review and invites the same decision twice. Both must remain in the register and in `MATRIX.md` for audit, and an explicit query must still find them.
- **Decision**: `idea_catcher.registry.list_ideas` hides rows whose status is `Rejected` and rows tagged `duplicate` unless the caller passes `include_rejected` / `include_duplicates`, asks for `status="Rejected"`, or asks for `tag="duplicate"`. The `ideas` CLI gains `--include-rejected` and `--include-duplicates`; its text output reports how many rows were hidden, while `--json` returns only the visible rows. `MATRIX.md` remains the full audit view. A reviewer may reject an idea that would harm the target directly, recording the reason and author through `set-status`.
- **Consequences**: Default target listings show only actionable ideas; rejected and duplicate rows stay auditable in `MATRIX.md` and through the explicit flags or filters. The filter is read-only and does not change the register schema. A caller that relied on the previous full listing must pass one or both flags.
- Cross-principle check: no principle section changed. Serves PRINCIPLE-000 (a closed idea is recorded, not silently deleted), PRINCIPLE-004 (the next cold start sees a shorter working list), PRINCIPLE-007 (a rejection needs a reason and author), PRINCIPLE-014 (unit and CLI tests cover every hidden kind and both opt-in paths), and PRINCIPLE-016 (the knowledge base names the new flags).

### ADR-265: Ordinary Git Push Asks for Confirmation; Force Push Stays Denied
- **Date**: 2026-10-29
- **Status**: Accepted
- **Context**: `opencode.jsonc` denied every `git push*` for every agent. A tested, user-authorized commit then had to be published by hand, and an agent that tried the normal delivery step hit a permission wall after Phase 4. The deny rule was meant to stop silent or destructive pushes, not to forbid the delivery step the operator explicitly authorized.
- **Decision**: `git push*` becomes `ask`, so the host asks the person before an ordinary push. `git push --force*`, `git push -f*`, `git push --mirror*` and `git push --delete*` stay `deny`. The permission-boundary test asserts the ask for ordinary pushes and the denial for force, mirror and delete pushes for every subagent.
- **Consequences**: An authorized commit can be published after a visible confirmation, while a subagent still cannot push silently or rewrite history. The operator sees the prompt and can refuse it. Every other protected edit and destructive shell command keeps its deny rule.
- Cross-principle check: no principle section changed. Serves PRINCIPLE-018 R1 (push delivery still needs authorization) and R7 (a verified commit is the one being pushed), PRINCIPLE-008 R6 (subagents still cannot push without confirmation), PRINCIPLE-014 (the permission test pins both the ask and the force denials), and PRINCIPLE-000 (the rule now matches the authorized delivery step instead of blocking it).

### ADR-266: Reforge Repairs Beside Uncommitted Work and Refuses Only a Dirty Commit Target
- **Date**: 2026-10-29
- **Status**: Accepted
- **Context**: A reviewed Reforge finding could not be applied because an unrelated file was uncommitted. The clean-tree gate protected another session's work, but it also made a completed review undeliverable: the user had to commit or stash work that Reforge would never touch. The same gate also refused a finding whose target file had a small, unrelated local edit, even though the exact replacement could be validated against that file and snapshotted before the attempt.
- **Decision**: `reforge/pipeline.py` captures every path that was already dirty before a repair, excluding its own state, ledger, artifacts and the findings file. Those paths are snapshotted with the finding targets, admitted to the patch review so they do not read as out-of-scope changes, and verified byte-for-byte after the repair; if one changed, the whole repair is rolled back. Repair and verification run even when a finding target is dirty, because the exact-once replacement and snapshot make that safe. Delivery is the only place that needs a clean target: `--commit`/`--push` refuses when a finding target was already dirty, unless `--allow-dirty` is passed, because `git add <path>` would otherwise publish the other session's edit. Paths Reforge repaired itself are remembered in the stage's repairs and do not count as foreign dirt, so a second `fix --commit` still publishes a reviewed patch.
- **Consequences**: A review can finish while another session edits unrelated files; Reforge never stages or commits those files, and a red test restores them too. Committing a target that had foreign work remains an explicit `--allow-dirty` decision. A repository that keeps its tree clean sees the previous behaviour.
- Cross-principle check: no principle section changed. Serves PRINCIPLE-006 (a reviewed patch is no longer blocked by unrelated work), PRINCIPLE-013 (snapshot and exact restore), PRINCIPLE-014 (new integration tests pin the dirty-unrelated and dirty-target paths), PRINCIPLE-018 R2 (explicit staging and no silent commit of foreign work), and PRINCIPLE-007 (a commit that would publish someone else's edit is refused with a Fix).

### ADR-267: Idea Include Flags Are Alternatives and Status Counts the Full Register
- **Date**: 2026-10-29
- **Status**: Accepted
- **Context**: ADR-264 hid `Rejected` ideas and `duplicate` tags from the default `ideas` listing, with one include flag per kind. A row that is both rejected and tagged duplicate needed both flags, although the README described the flags as alternatives. Separately, `status` kept using the filtered listing, so rejected and duplicate ideas silently disappeared from the register totals.
- **Decision**: `list_ideas` treats `include_rejected` and `include_duplicates` as alternatives: a row is shown when it is not hidden by any category, or when the requested category matches it. An explicit `status="Rejected"` or `tag="duplicate"` has the same effect for its category. `status` calls `list_ideas` with both include flags, so it reports the full register; `ideas` stays the filtered working list and keeps printing the hidden count. Unit tests pin a combined rejected/duplicate row and the full status totals.
- **Consequences**: Either flag now gives the complete view of its category, matching the documented behaviour; a caller that relied on needing both flags sees the extra row, which is the intended audit view. `status` no longer under-reports the register. The register schema and `MATRIX.md` are unchanged.
- Cross-principle check: no principle section changed. Serves PRINCIPLE-003 (the documented flag semantics and the code agree), PRINCIPLE-004 (cold-start status shows the real register), PRINCIPLE-007 (a combined hidden row is handled deliberately), PRINCIPLE-014 (unit and CLI tests cover the combination and the full counts), and PRINCIPLE-016 (README, architecture and the ADR register describe the behaviour).

### ADR-268: A Widening Stage Reviews Only the Commits It Adds
- **Date**: 2026-10-29
- **Status**: Accepted
- **Context**: A stage can add commits at both ends of the audited history: one older commit when the window widens, and new commits since the previous stage. Stage 2 of the first real run selected one old and four new commits, but `reforge/review.py` built the packet from the whole base..head range, so it repeated the entire stage-1 reforge refactor (201 KB) and would have re-reviewed files no new commit touched.
- **Decision**: `_changed_paths` and `_binary_paths` first collect the paths touched by `plan.new_shas`; a path that only an earlier stage changed is omitted from the packet and from the binary list. `findings_from_payload` validates a suggested replacement against the union of lines the new commits added, not against the wider base..head range. The diff for a file that both an earlier stage and a new commit touched remains the net base..head diff, so the reviewer still sees the file as it now stands.
- **Consequences**: A widening window reviews only its own changes, so a packet no longer grows with the whole audited history and cannot repeat stage-1 findings. A file changed by both old and new commits still shows one complete net diff; a path only the earlier stage changed is excluded. The packet is smaller and the patch-overlap check is narrower.
- Cross-principle check: no principle section changed. Serves PRINCIPLE-003 (findings are grounded in the commits the stage adds), PRINCIPLE-010 (a packet is bounded by the new work, not the whole window), PRINCIPLE-012 (less repeated input), and PRINCIPLE-014 (a unit test pins the prior-only path filter).

### ADR-269: A Fix Persists the Reviewed Status into the Stage History
- **Date**: 2026-10-29
- **Status**: Accepted
- **Context**: `run_pipeline` in `fix` mode updated `last_audit["review"]` and saved, but the matching entry in the run's `history` still held the earlier `unreviewed` review. When a later stage advanced the run, `report` showed an already-reviewed stage as unreviewed while `last_audit` said reviewed.
- **Decision**: Before saving a `fix` result, call `state.sync_last_audit(run)`, which updates the matching history entry in place, then save atomically. The existing stage key keeps the update on the same entry. A failing-first unit test runs `fix`, reloads the state and asserts both `last_audit.review` and `history[-1].review` say `reviewed`.
- **Consequences**: `report`, `status` and `last_audit` agree after a fix and after later stages; no schema or CLI change. A history entry whose timestamp/stage key does not match is left untouched, so an older stage is never relabelled.
- Cross-principle check: no principle section changed. Serves PRINCIPLE-003 (the visible record matches the stored one), PRINCIPLE-006 (checkpoint state survives a reload), PRINCIPLE-013 (the update is saved through the atomic writer), and PRINCIPLE-014 (the regression test pins both views).

### ADR-270: REUSE Metadata Reuses an Existing Lowercase licenses/ Directory
- **Date**: 2026-10-29
- **Status**: Accepted
- **Context**: `write_reuse_metadata` always targeted `LICENSES/`. On Windows the name is case-insensitive, so a project that already tracks legal texts in `licenses/` silently received the REUSE copy inside that lowercase directory, leaving a tracked path that a POSIX checkout resolves differently from the policy's `LICENSES/` expectation. The aegean-licensing project hit this during its Reforge delivery.
- **Decision**: `_reuse_licenses_dir` prefers an exact `LICENSES/` directory, then reuses an existing `licenses/` directory matched case-insensitively, and only then creates `LICENSES/`. `write_reuse_metadata` writes through that helper. A unit test covers the lowercase-directory case and the generated `REUSE.toml` annotation.
- **Consequences**: A project has one deterministic license-text directory and no case split on Windows. Projects that already use `licenses/` keep their historical path and receive the REUSE copy there; new projects still get `LICENSES/`. No state or schema changes.
- Cross-principle check: no principle section changed. Serves PRINCIPLE-003 (the tracked path matches what the checkout can resolve), PRINCIPLE-005 (generated project docs and paths stay truthful), PRINCIPLE-013 (the text is written through the atomic generated-file writer), and PRINCIPLE-014 (a unit test pins the helper and write path).

### ADR-271: UI Adoption and a Mechanics Contract in the AIKit Project Protocol
- **Date**: 2026-10-29
- **Status**: Accepted
- **Context**: AIKit projects keep host instructions at the AIKit root, by design, so a project opened alone only sees `.aikit/` memory. UI projects were detected but not adopted: `ui_required` was opt-in and `fix` refreshed the identifier without writing a contract. Game and project mechanics had no declared headless entry point, so an agent could only test them through the UI and the UI had no guaranteed core to call.
- **Decision**: Keep host instruction files root-only. Add two framework contracts to `.aikit/project.json` and the cockpit. First, `fix` detects a UI project; when it has a test command it writes a conservative `.aikit/ui.json` (surface `app`, detected entry files plus `src/**`/`game.js`/`public/**`, the project test command as the fast proof) and sets `ui_required`. It never invents a proof when no test command exists, and `check` warns on a detected UI without a contract. Second, a project may declare `mechanics` with `core` (existing relative paths) and `test` (one command); `mechanics_required` makes the contract mandatory, `check` validates it, and `resolve_gates` adds the mechanics test as a gate so an agent and the UI use the same headless core. No project repository is rewritten by this ADR; existing projects adopt the contracts through their own `fix`.
- **Consequences**: New and repaired projects get a bounded UI contract without a hand-written path list, and a UI project with no test command is reported rather than given a fake proof. A game can prove its mechanics headlessly and the UI can import the same core. Existing projects keep their old behavior until `fix` is run; a declared or required mechanics contract is enforced from then on.
- Cross-principle check: no principle section changed. Serves PRINCIPLE-003 (the contract validates real paths and commands), PRINCIPLE-009 (one registry, one gate resolver, no second UI framework), PRINCIPLE-011 (a missing proof is a Why/Fix warning, not a guess), PRINCIPLE-014 (the mechanics command is a test gate), and PRINCIPLE-016 (README, architecture, services and data models name the new fields).

### ADR-272: A Reforge Packet Reviews Every Commit Hunk in Order
- **Date**: 2026-10-29
- **Status**: Accepted; supersedes the packet layout of ADR-268
- **Context**: The packet collapsed each changed file into one net diff from the commit before the stage to its last commit. A minute change introduced and partly corrected across several commits disappeared into the net result, so the reviewer could not catch the intermediate bug or the small fix an author made. The operator asked for a hunk-by-hunk pass over the commits.
- **Decision**: `reforge/review.py` iterates `plan.new_shas` oldest to newest and emits a `### commit <sha> <subject>` section for each commit. Within a commit it lists that commit's `changed_files`, skips deleted/bookkeeping/binary paths, and writes `_commit_diff` (`git diff parent..commit -- path`) rather than the stage net diff. The prompt says a later commit may correct an earlier hunk, so evidence must still quote the current file and a replacement must overlap a line a new commit added. Binary paths are listed once at the end. `findings_from_payload` keeps validating against `_changed_paths` and `_new_added_lines`, so a stale hunk cannot become a live finding or patch.
- **Consequences**: Every commit hunk is visible and ordered, so an intermediate regression is reviewable; a file changed in several commits appears once per commit, increasing packet size. The reviewer judges the current tree, not an old hunk. ADR-268's prior-only file filter still applies; only its net-diff layout is replaced.
- Cross-principle check: no principle section changed. Serves PRINCIPLE-003 (each finding remains tied to the current file and the new commits' lines), PRINCIPLE-010 (the packet is bounded by the new commits, not the whole history), PRINCIPLE-012 (the host sees the actual hunks, not a collapsed summary), and PRINCIPLE-014 (unit and integration tests pin commit order, repeated files and redaction).

### ADR-273: Hunk IDs, Commit Attribution and Optional Coverage in a Reforge Review
- **Date**: 2026-10-29
- **Status**: Accepted
- **Context**: Hunk-by-hunk visibility (ADR-272) was not enough for deeper analysis: every review finding was still attributed to the stage head, a reviewer could not name the hunk it read, and an answer could silently skip hunks while claiming a review.
- **Decision**: `reforge/review.py` splits each commit's file diff into stable hunk records (`<sha>:<path>#<n>`, new-side line range) and prints a hunk manifest plus one labelled hunk block. A finding may carry `commit` and `hunk`; when supplied, the commit must be one of the stage's new commits, the hunk must exist in the packet, and the hunk must belong to the finding's path and commit. The finding stores both fields. An answer may also carry `reviewed_hunks`; the review report compares it with the packet's hunk IDs and prints `reported/total`, missing and unknown counts. The fields are optional so older answers stay valid; their absence is visible rather than silently treated as full coverage.
- **Consequences**: Findings can be traced to the exact commit and hunk, and a partial review is visible in the CLI and state. A caller can still return the old path/line form; coverage is asserted only when the reviewer supplies it. The packet carries a manifest and hunk labels, so it is slightly larger.
- Cross-principle check: no principle section changed. Serves PRINCIPLE-003 (attribution is grounded in a real commit and hunk), PRINCIPLE-010 (the manifest makes packet work measurable), PRINCIPLE-012 (the reviewer can name the exact unit instead of restating a file), and PRINCIPLE-014 (unit tests pin commit/hunk rejection and coverage counts).

### ADR-274: Packet Files Are Namespaced by Run, Not Only Stage
- **Date**: 2026-10-29
- **Status**: Accepted
- **Context**: Starting run-2 at the same HEAD and stage as run-1 wrote `stage-1-<head>.md` again, replacing run-1's packet; run-1's stored packet path then showed run-2's review. The path had no run id.
- **Decision**: `packet_path` includes `plan.run_id`, producing `stage-<run-id>-<stage>-<head>.md`. The review report also counts only known hunk ids as reported coverage, so an unknown id cannot make `reported` exceed `total`.
- **Consequences**: Independent runs at the same commit keep separate packets, and coverage arithmetic is honest. Existing state entries keep their old path; only future packets use the namespaced name.
- Cross-principle check: no principle section changed. Serves PRINCIPLE-003 (the stored path identifies the right review), PRINCIPLE-010 (bounded generated files), and PRINCIPLE-014 (unit tests pin distinct paths and coverage counts).
